#6832. The Best Gifts for Wives That She’ll Never Forget

To inspire you, we asked the wives on our team what gifts they wanted this year.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Finding the perfect gift for your wife is an opportunity to show her you really get her. I tapped my fellow Women’s Health editors, many of whom are wives and girlfriends, for an honest take on what they’d genuinely want from their partners. Trust me: these picks deliver. Whether she’s crushing workouts or leveling up her self-care routine, these gifts will make her feel celebrated and supported.

Even if you’re not currently searching for the perfect anniversary or birthday gift, there’s never a wrong time to surprise the woman you love with something thoughtful. For example, if your wife enjoys yoga, she’ll love Stakt’s folding mat. Or, maybe she’s serious about her morning rituals? In that case, she’ll love this five-minute journal to inspire some mindfulness first thing in the a.m. Perhaps she’s always got her eye on the latest wellness and recovery tools, making a pick from Therabody sure to please.

Below, find the best gift ideas for her in 2026. From fitness gifts to stress-relief gifts, this list is full of awesome tokens of appreciation for your better half.

Some sneakers are made to withstand all her tough workout sessions. Others are made to look good, and these cult-favorite Veja sneakers fall into the latter category. The casual sneaker is perfect for wearing to brunch, errands, and even work. Stylist Rose Lauture says these are a gift your wife will always want to wear. “It’s the best everyday sneaker to add to her wardrobe.”

If your wife is a reader, chances are she loves curling up with a good book before bedtime. What does she love less? The back pain that comes with trying to sit up comfortably against a headboard, using only her normal pillow for support. The solution: This WH top-selling backrest pillow. It’s made with a teardrop design (where one end is thicker than the other) that aligns the back for proper posture and provides extra lumbar support.

Get her a whole lineup of perfumes instead of guessing which bottle she’ll like. This set includes samples from brands like Gucci and Jo Malone London, plus a voucher she can redeem for an eligible fragrance at no extra cost. Remember her runner-up for Valentine’s Day.

If your wife has a habit of unintentionally letting her coffee go cold before she’s able to drink it all, this Ember smart mug makes a great gift. It will lock in her ideal sipping temperature until the very last drop, keeping it warm all day while it’s on its charging coaster (only 90 minutes if it’s not). Your wife will appreciate just how much you care about perfecting her morning routine, even on the busiest weekdays.

This WH-bestselling fill-in-the-blank book is full of imaginative prompts that will inspire you to open up about what you love about your wife. The best part of this personalized gift? You can make it exactly what you want—fill it with cute romantic thoughts or naughty funny ones. She’ll thoroughly enjoy flipping through every page.

This bath bundle from Cozy Earth screams coastal serenity, and it will make her me-time that much better. “They have this really interesting woven texture that feels plush but also makes drying after the shower or bath really easy,” says lifestyle director Lindsay Geller, who’s obsessed with these towels. “I love the light blue color, especially because these towels don’t shed at all, so they always look brand new.” The bath bundle comes with two bath sheets, two bath towels, two hand towels, and four washcloths.

If she’s been complaining that her go-to exercise mat is starting to wear thin, the Stakt Mat makes an awesome replacement, and as a bonus, it’s probably more supportive than anything else she’s already got lying around. “It’s thicker than typical mats and folds rather than rolls, so it’s quick and comfortable to use for yoga, resistance training, and Pilates workouts,” says WH fitness contributor Talene Appleton. It’s especially great for those with tender knees, since it’s easier on the joints, she adds.

Wives on the go need headphones that can keep up. That’s just what this sporty option from Bose does without sacrificing any sound quality. They’re the perfect gift for the wife who regularly commutes to work and/or trains outdoors. Worth noting: They don’t go into her ears, allowing her to hear the world around her while still listening to her favorite playlist with crystal-clear clarity.

Pajamas should be effortlessly cozy without overlooking style. And that’s precisely what this set does. It's made with soft and stretchy jersey fabric that hardly wrinkles.

We can’t imagine a better evening for the ultra-competitive wife—especially if she loves true crime—than this immersive game. It’s a WH bestseller because players get to take on the role of detective and try to solve a murder mystery. With codes that need to be cracked and realistic motives that need analyzing, it’s like living out your own true crime podcast.

Give that ice pack she’s got in the freezer a tech-y upgrade. This portable recovery tool from Therabody, which is a brand beloved by WH readers, offers cold and heat therapy wherever she needs it. The palm-sized, wearable pad comes with three preset options—cold, hot, and a blend of both—that can be used to treat sore muscles or other pain, like period cramps.

Brand hype aside, there’s so much to like about this Dyson hair dryer. It’s a favorite among WH editors with different hair types, who all swear by it for a super-fast blowout with minimal heat. Our beauty editors say it’s worth every penny, especially since it comes with five attachments—a diffuser, a wide-tooth comb, a flyaway attachment, a styling concentrator, and a gentle air attachment. It’s a splurge, but a failsafe gift you can’t go wrong with.

Named the best budget option in our best sweatpants list, these sweats are for both lounging and getting movement in, even if it’s just running to catch her next flight. It’s designed with a cropped leg and relaxed fit, and of course, it’s stretchy and moisture-wicking, too.

The Everywhere Belt Bag is single-handedly keeping fanny packs in style, at least that’s the consensus among WH editors. The small bag is perfect for storing everyday essentials like keys, lip gloss, cell phones, and other small items. Even better, it’s always available in an endless array of cute colors and seasonal styles.

Yes, this IG-famous pan is worth the hype. The ceramic nonstick surface cleans up easily, and she can use it for everything from morning eggs to stir-fries, plus steamed dumplings with the included basket. It comes in plenty of fun colors, so pick one that suits her kitchen.

A weekend getaway is all the better with the right duffel—preferably one that meets your wife’s every packing need without weighing her (or your car) down. Enter Etronik. It has plenty of space for several days of ‘fits, a hidden shoe compartment, and a padded shoulder strap that makes it more comfy to carry. As a bonus, it’s easy to clean.

These cozy memory foam loafers are a low-key treat your wife will actually wear around the house and after workouts. The plush lining hugs her feet, and the cushy insole gives just the right support for lounging or light errands.

A diamond necklace doesn’t have to be an anniversary-only purchase. The simple round solitaire is a foolproof choice, and opting for a lab-grown diamond keeps the cost down, even at a full carat. Check whether she usually wears yellow or white metals, then choose the finish to match.

That favorite photo of you two deserves better than getting buried in her camera roll. Turn it into a keepsake with this brass-and-glass ornament, which includes a double-sided print, then then add the optional year tag to mark the occasion.

For some people, there’s no such thing as too many skin care gadgets. If that sounds familiar, gift accordingly with one of WH’s favorite facial wands. This tool utilizes four different functions to boost blood circulation and spark cell turnover that results in more glowy skin.

#6827. The 6 Best Pillows of 2026, Tested by Editors

The $190 Purple Harmony Pillow might be divisive, but it earned our best overall badge.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

As someone with frequent neck pain, I would give up my cozy sheets, white noise machine, and sunrise alarm clock if it meant I wouldn’t wake up with a stiff neck. (And that’s coming from someone who invests a lot in their sleep.)

"I think that a pillow can make a big difference in people that have a history of neck issues," says Leia Rispoli, MD, an interventional pain management specialist and physiatrist at DISC Sport & Spine Center. This is the very reason why I tend to lug my favorite pillow around on weekend trips.

There isn’t one pillow that works for everyone. Your best option depends on your sleep position, preferred firmness, body type, and whether you tend to overheat at night. To find the best pillows, our editors and testers slept on popular models from Purple, Casper, Coop, Saatva, and other leading sleep brands. We evaluated each one for comfort, support, temperature regulation, adjustability, ease of care, and value.

I love the Purple Harmony Pillow so much that I try to convert nearly everyone who mentions sleep within earshot of me. I even pack it for overnight trips. But it does feel unusual at first. Inside, a springy, ventilated latex core is wrapped in Purple’s GelFlex Grid, a flexible, honeycomb-shaped layer that bends under the weight of your head and springs back when you move. Together, these materials make the pillow feel buoyant rather than sink-in like memory foam.

I tested the medium, 6.5-inch loft, meaning the pillow’s height before it is compressed. It provided enough height to support my head and neck while I slept on my side without feeling rigid. The materials give slightly beneath heavier areas, which helped reduce uncomfortable pressure around my head and neck. As someone who frequently experiences neck and back pain, I consistently felt comfortable and supported while sleeping on it.

Purple recommends the low, 5.5-inch version for most stomach and back sleepers. The medium and tall versions are generally better suited to side and combination sleepers, who often need more height to fill the space between the head and mattress. Body size and personal preference will also affect which height feels most comfortable.

The Harmony was also one of the coolest pillows I tested. Air circulates through the ventilated latex and open honeycomb layer instead of becoming trapped inside a solid block of foam. Its stretchy outer cover is also designed to draw sweat away from the skin so it can evaporate. Even on warmer nights, I rarely felt the need to flip the pillow over in search of a cooler surface.

After nearly three years of regular use, my pillow has not developed noticeable lumps or gone flat. Its durable build helps justify the high price, but there are compromises. The pillow is unusually heavy, and some sleepers may notice the grid’s texture through the cover. Purple’s 30-night trial is also shorter than the trial periods offered by many competing brands.

Still, no other pillow I tested delivered the same combination of responsive support, cooling, and long-term shape retention. I would purchase it again without hesitation.

Women’s Health art director Christine Giordano typically sleeps with two or three pillows, but the Casper Original gave her enough support to use fewer. “This pillow gave me the support I needed while still feeling light and airy,” she says. “It was breathable and didn’t clump, keeping me cool and comfortable at night.”

The Casper uses a pillow-within-a-pillow design: A firmer inner cushion provides structure, while a softer outer layer adds cushioning. Both layers are filled with polyester microfiber, a synthetic material made to feel light and fluffy like down. The construction prevented Giordano’s head from sinking too deeply while still giving the pillow a plush feel.

Giordano tries to sleep on her back but usually shifts onto her left side during the night. The pillow remained comfortable and supportive in both positions, making it a strong choice for combination sleepers who alternate between their backs and sides. However, stomach sleepers or people who prefer a flatter pillow may find its approximately nine-inch uncompressed height too lofty.

The breathable cotton cover and airy synthetic fill also prevented the pillow from feeling stuffy during testing. It’s not as breathable as a dedicated cooling pillow, but Giordano did not notice heat buildup or need to flip it over for a cooler surface.

The entire pillow is machine-washable, which makes it easier to clean than models with foam or latex cores. Its soft filling may need occasional fluffing to maintain its shape, but Giordano did not notice clumping during testing.

At under $100, it is one of the more affordable pillows we tested. Giordano liked it enough that she replaced some of the other pillows in her home and purchased additional Casper ones for her family.

Women’s Health executive health and fitness director Jacqueline Andriakos, CPT, calls the Coop Original Adjustable the most comfortable pillow she has tried. “I’ve never been a pillow snob or had major issues with basic pillows from Amazon, but I noticed that this was far more comfortable than other, cheaper pillows I’ve used,” she says.

The pillow is filled with a blend of shredded memory foam and microfiber. Memory foam molds around the head and neck, while the soft synthetic fibers keep the pillow from feeling like a dense, solid block of foam. Unlike a pillow with a fixed height, the Coop can be unzipped so you can remove filling for a flatter feel or add more for extra support. The process can get messy, but that adjustability makes it easier to tailor the pillow to your body and sleep position.

Coop offers the pillow in classic, crescent, and cut-out shapes. Andriakos tested the classic and cut-out versions while switching between side and stomach sleeping. She preferred the cut-out, which has a curved indentation that creates room for the shoulder and allows the pillow to sit closer to the neck.

“I liked that it could wrap around my neck and shoulders, and I could almost cuddle it like a body pillow with an arm threaded underneath,” she says. Although the shape can look slightly awkward inside a standard pillowcase, it was super comfortable when she slept on her side.

The pillow felt plush, but Andriakos’ head did not sink so deeply that her neck felt unsupported. She also found the outer cover breathable, although sleepers who regularly overheat may prefer a pillow specifically designed for cooling.

Because its height can be adjusted, the Coop can work for side, back, stomach, and combination sleepers. Side sleepers will generally need more filling to keep the head level with the spine, while stomach sleepers may prefer to remove enough filling to create a flatter surface. Finding the right amount may require a few nights of experimentation, but Coop’s 100-night trial gives you time to make those adjustments.

It would take an exceptional pillow for me to consider replacing my Purple Harmony, but the Saatva Latex Pillow gave it serious competition. Although both pillows contain latex, they feel noticeably different. Purple uses one solid, springy latex core, while Saatva uses shredded pieces of latex surrounded by a soft layer of fine synthetic fibers. This gives the Saatva a plusher, more traditional feel, but it has enough structure to support the head and neck.

The pillow feels dense when you first pick it up, but my head settled gently into the soft outer layer without sinking too deeply. The shredded latex inside quickly returned to its original shape when I moved, so the pillow stayed supportive as I shifted positions. As a side sleeper who frequently experiences neck pain, I did not wake up with additional stiffness.

Saatva offers two heights. The standard version measures approximately four to five inches and is designed for back and stomach sleepers who generally need a lower pillow. The high-loft version measures approximately six to seven inches and is better suited to side and combination sleepers who need more height between their heads and mattresses.

I also appreciated that the pillow remained breathable throughout the night. The small spaces between the pieces of shredded latex allow air to circulate, while the cotton cover feels cool. It does not create the immediately cold sensation of a cooling pillow, but I did not notice uncomfortable heat buildup.

The outer layer is filled with microdenier fibers, which are very fine synthetic strands designed to imitate the light, fluffy feel of down. You can machine-wash this outer layer and its cotton cover, but the inner latex core must be removed and kept out of the washing machine.

At $165 for a standard/queen size, this is one of the more expensive pillows we tested. Saatva also provides only 45 days for returns, which is shorter than the trial periods offered by several competitors. Still, its combination of plush cushioning, responsive support, and breathable materials makes it an excellent choice for sleepers who want a luxury-hotel feel that’s still supportive.

For hot sleepers, a breathable pillow can be the difference between a good night’s sleep and a night spent tossing and turning. The Slumber Cloud UltraCool stood out because its cooling effect was immediately noticeable during testing.

Women’s Health managing editor Laura McLaughlin described the sensation as “a little sci-fi” because the surface felt immediately cool when she rested her head on it. The cover contains Outlast, a material designed to absorb excess body heat when you become warm and release it as your skin cools. This helps prevent heat from collecting around your head and face, although the effect may be less noticeable beneath a thick pillowcase.

McLaughlin also found the pillow easy to combine with others. “It’s great for layering with other pillows to get just the right height,” she says. “It’s neither too dense nor too high to use in combination with others already in place.” That makes it especially useful for sleepers who want additional height without placing a rigid pillow beneath their heads.

The interior is filled with polyester down alternative, a synthetic material designed to imitate the airy feel of down. It is available in soft/medium and medium/firm versions, allowing sleepers to choose how plush or structured they want the pillow to feel. The softer option will generally suit stomach sleepers or anyone who prefers more sink while the firmer version may provide better support for back and side sleepers.

The entire pillow can be machine-washed, a convenient advantage over pillows with foam or latex cores. However, synthetic filling can begin to clump after repeated use and washing, so it may require regular fluffing to redistribute the material.

Although the UltraCool does not provide the customized support of an adjustable pillow, it delivered the most noticeable cooling sensation of any model we tested.

The Brooklinen Down Alternative Pillow is built for sleepers who want the fluffy feel of down without actual feathers. It’s filled with recycled poly microgel, a cluster of fine synthetic fibers that creates a light cushioning similar to down.

Unlike many pillows that come in only one firmness, this one is available in soft, medium, and firm versions. Brooklinen recommends the soft pillow for stomach sleepers who generally need less height beneath the head; the medium for back sleepers; and the firm for side sleepers who typically need more support to fill the space between the head and mattress.

Its cotton sateen shell feels smooth and has a subtle sheen. The pillow is also certified by OEKO-TEX Standard 100, which means its materials have been tested for a list of regulated substances. That certification does not evaluate the pillow’s comfort, durability, or environmental impact.

Brooklinen allows most products to be returned within 365 days, although it deducts a $9.95 return fee from the refund. That’s one of the most generous return windows we’ve seen.

The pillow can be machine-washed in cold water and tumble-dried on low with wool dryer balls until completely dry. As with most pillows filled with loose synthetic fibers, it may require regular fluffing to redistribute the fill and preserve its shape.

A pillow’s height, or loft, should keep your head and neck aligned with your spine. Side sleepers generally need a taller pillow, back sleepers a medium-height pillow, and stomach sleepers a low pillow. Your mattress matters, too: The more deeply your shoulders sink into it, the less pillow height you may need. Most of the pillows we tested fell in the five- to nine-inch range, offering different levels of support depending on their material and sleeping position.

Side sleepers often benefit from a medium-firm or firm pillow that maintains its height. Back sleepers generally need medium support while stomach sleepers may prefer something softer that compresses easily. Because firmness labels vary by brand, consider the pillow’s material and height as well.

“There isn't one brand that's the magical brand for everyone,” says Rispoli. Look for a pillow that supports your head without tilting your neck sharply upward or downward. Adjustable pillows can help you fine-tune the height. Consult a health care professional if your pain is persistent, severe, or accompanied by numbness or weakness.

Memory foam molds around the head but may retain heat. Latex feels springy, breathable, and durable. Down is soft and airy but may lack structure while synthetic down alternative is usually easier to wash but may flatten over time. Hybrid pillows combine materials to balance cushioning and support.

Look for ventilated latex, shredded filling, or another construction that allows air to circulate. Some covers also absorb excess body heat or feel cool, although a thick pillowcase may reduce the effect. People who sleep cold may prefer denser foam or plush down filling that holds slightly more warmth.

As a sleep writer, I’ve tested all kinds of sleep accessories, from mattresses to eye masks, but pillows are probably my most-tested product category. (Unfortunately, I once tested so many pillows at the same time that I injured my neck, but I’ve learned my lesson.) To find the best pillows available online, I selected popular picks from leading sleep brands, like Purple, Casper, Saatva, Pluto, and more, for testing. Some helpful testers and I have slept on these pillows for dozens of nights to determine whether they’re worth the money, and our top pick has been my favorite pillow to sleep on for nearly three years. From flat pillow lovers to hot sleepers, our diverse crew of sleepers tried out eight pillows of various styles, materials, and price points.

#6824. The Best Walking Shoe Black Friday Deals: Save Up to 61% on Sneakers Our Editors Swear By

Shop favorites from Brooks, New Balance, Dr. Scholl’s, and more.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Walking is a low-impact workout that boosts your heart rate, supports mental health, and is far easier on your joints than running. The catch? All those miles still add up, which means your shoes really matter.

Over the years, our team of editors has tested hundreds of shoes, so we can recommend only the best styles for walking, running, cross-training, and more. Supportive, cushioned walking shoes aren’t cheap, and many of our editors’ favorites cost more than $100—but you don’t have to pay full price because our editors found Black Friday discounts on some of our top picks.

I’m a shopping editor who tracks deals for a living, and right now, you can save up to 61 percent on top-performing brands like Brooks, New Balance, and more. With proven cushioning, stability, and comfort, shop the shoe deals that Women’s Health editors genuinely wear and recommend below.

After testing dozens of walking shoes for our 2026 Fitness Awards, the Adrenaline GTS 25 took the top spot for its near-perfect balance between cushioning and stability. That comfort starts with the 25’s updated midsole, which feels plusher than previous models without losing an ounce of stability. It still features the brand’s signature GuardRails technology—firmer along the sides and softer through the center—to gently lock in your stride.

The upper and outsole of the Glycerin Flex are both made from super bendy materials that give the shoe an almost barefoot feel. We’re big fans of this shoe for walking, long periods of standing, running, and plyometric workouts. It's incredibly versatile, thanks to ample nitrogen-infused cushioning and a flexible midsole. The FlexZone technology—a strategically placed groove in the center of the shoe’s outsole—helps enhance the shoe’s flexibility and allows the foot to move naturally.

If versatility is your priority, the Brooks Ghost 17 delivers. With upgraded nitrogen-fused cushioning, the latest version packs even more padding than the Ghost 16. Impressively, the higher heel stack doesn’t feel too squishy or wobbly. It’s a great do-it-all shoe for long walks, standing all day, and running.

The soft midsole offers the just-right level of give, absorbing impact without feeling too soft or marshmallowy. Plus, the breathable air mesh upper adds to the barely-there feel, and the plush ankle collar doesn’t chafe or rub at all.

This TikTok-famous sneaker reigns supreme for walking and has earned a spot in our guides to the best platform sneakers and best travel shoes for good reason: it delivers all-day comfort without looking overly athletic. The 1.5-inch platform gives this shoe major style points, elongating the legs and giving a little extra height without that heavy, clunky feel some platform sneakers have.

Plantar fasciitis and the heel pain that comes with it can quickly halt your walking workouts. The 2025 WH Fitness Award-winning, best overall plantar fasciitis walking shoe, Brooks Women’s Adrenaline GTS 24, delivers stability and support to keep you (and your plantar fascia) feeling good over time.

The New Balance 608 V5 Trainer has over 38,000 Amazon reviews, and for good reason. The brand integrated its signature ABZORB midsole, designed to reduce joint impact during walking through its cushioning and compression resistance. Plus, they come in narrow, standard, and wide sizing.

For those who appreciate a good slip-on shoe, the Skechers Go Walk Flex Azriel’s lightweight, breathable mesh upper is great for sweaty feet. The ultra-cushioned midsole also offers great arch support.

"This is a solid no-frills walking shoe that’s perfect for errands, quick hot girl strolls, or for walking your dog," says associate style and reviews editor Shannen Zitz. "I love that it slips on easily while still having the appearance of a classic sneaker with bungee laces that never come undone.”

If your main problem is overpronation or supination, the New Balance 1540v3 will lock your feet down. The brand integrates its "Rollbar stability post system," designed to prevent your ankles and feet from rolling inward or outward. On top of that, dense foam is in the midsole for added arch support, while the collar is tough for ankle security. Despite its firmness, the EVA foam remains plush. Extra-wide sizing is available to accommodate a variety of foot types (in addition to narrow, standard, and wide).

Diana Rogers, DPM, a podiatric foot and ankle surgery specialist in Crystal Lake, Illinois, and Casey Pidich, MD, a podiatrist and foot and ankle surgeon based in the Bronx, New York, both recommend Hoka's Clifton series to patients for arch support, so you know it's a good one.

Pidich loves that the design encourages great form—the sole ensures that your feet rock off the ground rather than push off your toes. Rogers specifically recommends the shoe to her patients with plantar fasciitis (a condition that causes arch pain) for this exact reason. "The lightweight feel of this shoe propels feet forward rather than making them do extra work," she says.

Lined with mesh for ultra-breathable comfort, the Pegasus is a winner thanks to its slim, low heel that helps maintain a smooth walking cadence. We love that the 42 is lighter than the previous model and has a curvier toe box that gives more toe spring, a smoother ride, and a roomier fit.

#6821. Best Black Friday Apple Deals: Save Up to 35% on AirPods, iPads, and More

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Whether we’re popping in a pair of AirPods before a long run or checking our step count on our Apple Watches, we can’t resist Apple’s gear—especially while trying to hit our fitness goals. Our editors have used and tested Apple tech for years, so we know what’s worth your money and what’s not.

Amazon is discounting new models on top-tier tech for Black Friday and Cyber Monday. As a shopping editor who writes about sales for a living, I’ve been watching for notable markdowns, and right now, you can score iPads, AirPods, and Apple Watches up to 35 percent off.

The AirPods Pro 3 are discounted by 20 percent, and WH Executive Health & Fitness Director Jacqueline Andriakos reviewed the earbuds’ heart rate and fitness-tracking features. “The AirPods Pro 3’s in‑ear heart‑rate sensing is definitely a revelation for those days [when I forget my Apple Watch]: I can head out the door, get a workout in, and still get the HR and fitness tracking data (such as calorie burn) even when I don’t have my wearable on my wrist,” she writes.

#6816. What to Wear to Thanksgiving: 6 Outfit Ideas for Every Type of Gathering

From dressier holiday looks to comfy and stretchy.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

With Thanksgiving just around the corner, I’ve already started thinking about two very important things: what’s going on the dinner table and what I’ll wear. I’m especially looking forward to my mom’s baked mashed potatoes (a family favorite) and the homemade mac and cheese I’ll be bringing along. But, especially as a style writer, half the fun of the holidays is having a good excuse to put together a good outfit.

My family’s Thanksgiving gatherings tend to be pretty casual, so I’m not one to show up in anything overly dressy or uncomfortable. I’m usually somewhere in the middle, opting for an outfit that looks like I made an effort but still lets me enjoy a second helping of stuffing without worrying about a too-tight waistband. Think a great sweater with loose-fitting jeans, a knit dress with boots, or even a smartly-styled leggings outfit. Because as much as I love dressing up for the holidays, I also want to enjoy post-dinner card games or sit comfortably on the couch afterward.

If you’re approaching Thanksgiving dressing the same way, I’ve rounded up six outfit ideas to help get you inspired. I’ve included a few options perfect for a Christmas card photoshoot, along with more laid-back looks that are just as fitting for a casual family gathering. No matter what your holiday plans look like, these outfits strike that happy medium between looking good and feeling comfortable. Now, if you’ll excuse me, I have some mac and cheese to make.

In my opinion, there’s no outfit more perfect for the holidays than a sweater dress and boots. It’s cute enough for family photos but loose and soft enough to keep you comfy all day (especially if you kick the boots off at the door). Grab a long-sleeve knit dress that hits somewhere between mid-thigh and the knee and pair it with some fleece-lined tights and your fave knee-high boot. A midi or maxi dress with an ankle bootie works too.

Start by pulling out a denim jacket and denim skirt. From there, add a plain white T-shirt. I love brown for the fall, so I wore my version of this outfit with brown boots and a matching brown belt and bag. I love this combo because it looks super stylish, but I don’t feel overdressed or uncomfortable when I wear it. Don’t have a denim skirt or jacket? No problem. You can recreate this look with a leather jacket, jeans, or any neutral mini skirt.

Start with a pair of satin or silk pants and pair them with a sweater and ballet flats in the same shade. This outfit has been one of my go-tos lately because it’s so comfortable, but the monochrome palette and shiny finish of the pants make it look like I tried way harder than I actually did. You can even make it feel a little dressier by swapping the flats for a pair of pointed-toe ankle boots.

It doesn’t get comfier than leggings and slippers. For ultra-casual gatherings when you just want to focus on family and food without looking frumpy, just style yours a little smarter. Go for a pair of leggings with minimal seaming (especially a pair without a front seam), which makes them look sleeker. Then grab a dressy-looking oversized sweater—something with a boxy cut, a turtleneck, or a nice knit. I find that my tweed stitch J. Crew Rollneck Sweater is perfect for this look. Finish it off with a neutral slipper and a pair of scrunchy socks.

Crz Yoga SculptAir No Front Seam Leggings

Opt for a pair of baggier jeans for this more casual outfit, which will be way comfier than a restrictive skinny or straight-leg jean. A looser pant also pairs better with a cozy boot, like this platform ankle boot. Finally, throw on a long-sleeve top or sweater with a cute vest for an outfit that feels very fall.

Banana Republic The Icon Classic Wide-Leg Jean

Okay, you caught me—I actually wore this outfit for Christmas last year instead of Thanksgiving, but it definitely works for either holiday! For this combo, start with your favorite skirt and a turtleneck top. Then top it off with a relaxed-fitting blazer (I love this houndstooth blazer, but any pattern or solid color will do). On the bottom, throw on a pair of tights and a knee-high boot.

#6812. The 7 Best Facial Hair Removal Tools, Tested by Editors

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

There are tons of different facial hair removal methods for women, including laser hair removal, waxing, and epilators. I have tried them all, including electric razors and dermaplaning tools. As WH’s beauty editor, I've sugared my face and body and interviewed dozens of derms about preventing dreaded razor bumps and unsightly ingrown hairs. Some facial hair removal methods take only a few minutes, while others can be pretty painful but require minimal upkeep. Finding the right method or combination of methods that work for your needs might take a few tries, but I've got you covered with my beauty editor-vetted recommendations. These editor-tested and dermatologist-recommended suggestions start at just $10.

Yes, this is the priciest recommendation, but it's totally worth the splurge. Braun works in just one treatment session and boasts smart features that justify the added cost and our top spot. The results are immediate, long-lasting, and include three attachments for the face, bikini line, and rest of the body.

Skin i·expert is Braun’s only laser made with a proprietary SensoAdapt technology that scans your skin tone and automatically adjusts the light intensity for an effective and safer hair removal experience. The software is controlled with the Braun smartphone app. It also sends alerts when you missed a spot, used the wrong device head, applied too much pressure, or used it too quickly.

Wohlner relies on this device every few weeks to remove her self-described "super coarse hair." Since laser hair removal isn't permanent, some hairs will still pop up. However, they grow back slower, which is a major win for Wohlner.

Laser hair removal also gets a pretty bad rep for being painful. While the Braun isn't equipped with any cooling sensation to relieve discomfort, the pain is relatively mild. "I felt a little zippiness here and there, but nothing I could actually describe as painful—more like a super quick, tiny pinch," says Wohlner.

As for the results, they were solid. Wohlner didn't have to shave her hair for weeks after the first treatment, and three sessions later, her hair regrowth slowed down. The upfront cost is pricey, but it'll save you from purchasing expensive pro-grade appointments and allow you to remove hair whenever and wherever you choose.

Read More: Electrolysis Versus Laser Hair Removal

Finishing Touch is an underrated brand. I previously included the brand’s electric shaver with a built-in LED ring light to illuminate hairs I wouldn't usually see in my bathroom. However, it’s no longer being sold and cost more than double the price. This slimmer version is the ideal facial hair removal tool for budget-conscious shoppers, and I wish I ordered mine sooner.

I can achieve similar results using razors three times the price. The Flawless device is also so much prettier than boring razors. The rose gold hair remover is rather discreet and looks similar to dermaplaning tool. It features a small head that contours along the curves of my face, while the blade ensures I have the smoothest trim.

This device works great if you have sensitive skin that's easily irritated. It's gentle enough to use daily, and I love keeping mine on hand at the office to check my face before walking into a meeting.

The only caveat is Flawless is a razor, so it cuts hair at the top layer of skin, meaning hair will grow back relatively fast. So, as long as you don't mind regular trims, this is an epic choice.

After two years of on-and-off testing, Flamingo still makes my favorite razor. Its sharp blades that don’t tug or pull at my sensitive skin elevate Flamingo to the top of my shower routine.

The brand suggests replacing the blades every four to six weeks, but each blade lasts me closer to eight weeks, meaning a regular four-pack of blades lasts me approximately 32 weeks (eight months) before becoming dull. Plus, this razor set is comfy to use while shaving down to my ankles. It curves along my kneecaps and pits with ease, and it hasn’t caused any nicks or ingrown hairs along my bikini line.

I’ll continue using Flamingo, and at just $16, you can also enjoy smooth skin which includes the razor, four blades, and a holder that fits neatly in cramped spaces like my New York City shower.

While I don't mind using a traditional manual razor or dermaplaning, it's worth spending a little extra on this electric device, says Wohlner. This Philips electric razor has a large head and self-sharpening blade to rapidly remove facial hair. Wohlner's been using this facial hair removal tool for hairs and considers it the most effective method for eliminating stubborn upper lip and chin hairs. "While it's too big to remove hair on my brows, it's awesome for those larger areas of the face when you're short on time and want to look smooth and stubble-free," she says.

Many other tools can irritate, especially around the upper lip area, which occasionally turns into bumps. This shaver has a hypoallergenic stainless steel head that doesn’t irritate sensitive skin, quickly removing unwanted hair in a single pass without causing pain or discomfort. This device keeps Wohlner's face smooth, and it always glides over the skin effortlessly without leaving it red and raw. It even comes with a built-in mirror in the cap to touch-up on the go.

Pro tip: The large shaver head does tend to get clogged after a few uses, which can be prevented if you wipe it down with alcohol after every use. Taking care of your device will ensure you get the closest shave and that it lasts for years.

The idea of gliding a sharp razor across my face was initially intimidating, but I haven't scratched myself since using the Dermaflash. Unlike cheaper options with dull blades, the Dermaflash provides the most professional-grade shave I've ever experienced while testing dozens of dermaplaning tools. The sharp blade features proprietary Microfine Edges, which are tiny corrugations that get super close to my face without any discomfort.

Most dermaplaning tools only have simple blades, but Dermaflash features a gentle pulsing technology that further removes peach fuzz and dead skin cells instantly and safely, making it handy for beginners and seasoned dermaplaning users alike. My results rival pro treatments without the hassle of going to a spa and without the excessive upkeep cost. This tool easily glides across my face, which would normally be a challenge with cheaper, manual dermaplaning tools. Since using this device, I noticed my face cream sinks in quickly, and my foundation looks even more natural.

I try to use this tool every two weeks to maintain my smooth results, but TBH, I use it monthly, which is better for my wallet. The brand advises swapping out the blade after every use for the smoothest results, so anything to stretch my beauty budget is always a win. FWIW: Even a monthly treatment prevents my makeup from pilling, so this tool will remain a consistent part of my routine.

Sugaring is epic because the paste only sticks to the hair, not the skin. It's far less painful than waxing and easier to apply.

I've previously only done professional sugaring, but the at-home process is the same. After heating the roller in the microwave, I apply the stick directly to the area I want sugared, lay down a strip, and pull it off in the opposite direction of the hair growth. The fabric strips are a little big to use on my face, especially on the chin or upper lip, but I can cut them in half, and they work perfectly.

Most sugaring wax is packaged in a tub that can tip over and cause a huge, sticky mess. Schick comes in a deodorant-like stick that easily rolls onto my skin. I like to warm the stick up in the microwave in 5-second increments until the paste has a honey-like consistency. I also need a light layer of sugaring wax to achieve the same results as other alternatives, meaning my roller can last for a few months.

I initially missed a few hairs, so I had to follow up with a fresh swipe of sugaring paste. With some practice, my technique improved over time, and soon enough, it only took me a few minutes to wax the lower half of my face. At just $13 a wand, this at-home technique is much cheaper than going to a salon where I'd easily pay double the price, and for that alone, I'll continue using this kit.

This hair removal cream isn't the Nair you grew up on. It's a prescription-strength cream specifically formulated to slow hair growth on the face. The key ingredients include eflornithine (6%), which blocks the enzyme ornithine decarboxylase (ODC) that stimulates hair growth. Each batch is compounded together shortly before shipping, ensuring maximum potency.

I've tried many hair removal creams, which sometimes cause redness and irritation, so I was worried Musely would be a problem. Musely provides instant hair-free results, and I noticed my regrowth is slower and my skin remains smoother with regular use, compared to other creams I’ve tried. It has a light tingling sensation, letting me know the cream is working. Unlike cheaper drugstore alternatives, Musely hasn't irritated my skin. It also doesn't have a super aggressive chemical smell like other alternatives I tested, and that alone is a win in my book.

The major downside of hair removal creams, including Musely, is that I have to adjust when I use my tretinoin prescription around hair removal days. After Musely hair removal, I typically wait a few days before safely applying my tretinoin cream. Otherwise, I might experience redness, irritation, and over-exfoliate my skin. Musely is a worthwhile product, but I prefer dermaplaning or shaving because I value acne-free skin over a fast-acting, pain-free hair removal process.

Your hair color, skin color, and hair thickness helps determine your best facial hair removal method, says dermatologist Blair Murphy-Rose, MD. Also be real about your pain tolerance and bandwidth because the best method for removing facial hair is the one you'll consistently use to maintain smooth results. Also, think about investing in an electric hair removal device to speed up your primping routine and, of course, stick to your budget. Our suggestions start at just $10.

They are epic when you have dark hair and a fair to medium complexion; particularly those with thick, coarse and unruly dark chin, upper lip, and sideburn hair. "This combination of hair color and skin tone ensures that the hairs alone are absorbing the majority of the laser energy," dermatologist Rachel Nazarian, MD,,, recently told WH.

But if you're a four or deeper on the Fitzpatrick Scale like me, you should skip using at-home laser hair removal devices. "You want to receive the right treatment that does not harm your skin," dermatologist Jeanine Downie, MD, previously told WH.

They work by slowing hair growth and reducing strand thickness by using highly concentrated light to penetrate hair follicles and inhibit future hair growth. While laser hair removal isn't exactly permanent, it can effectively slow hair growth. While the upfront cost is a tad of a jumpscare, these devices typically contain about 200,000 flashes, which is equivalent to 780 at-home treatments, says Dr. Nazarian.

This is my go-to hair removal method because of the low upkeep. I can easily go 4-6 weeks between my sessions, but TBH, I'll stretch it to every 8 weeks. It is relatively painful since it involves simultaneously removing dozens of hairs from the root, but it's a quick process and the results last longer than dermaplaning or shaving. I've been waxing for years and find the pain has become quite tolerable. On a scale of 1 to 10, waxing my face and underarms ranges from 2—3 and 4—6 elsewhere on my body, with the most painful spots being the back of my legs and bikini area. Sugaring It is similar to waxing but less painful. I've had some uncomfy waxes that stung my sensitive skin, but sugaring is noticeably milder. Ingredients will vary among brands, but there is almost always a mix of sugar, water, and lemon. The golden paste is molded onto your skin where you'd like to remove hair, and you'll gently remove it with a fast flicking technique like waxing.

These are tried-and-true hair removal tools that are cheap, readily accessible, and provide instant results. Your best bet is to use a razor with a non-slip ergonomic handle to keep your shave steady. I also like razors featuring a lubricating strip to help prevent irritation. Depending on how fast your facial hair regrows, you might need to shave daily or every few days, but they're pain-free. Remember to use shaving cream and gently run the blade in the opposite direction of hair growth. There are also manual and electric razors, but I'll discuss that more in the next section.

They feature surgical blades to remove dead skin cells and peach fuzz from your face. These devices also have long handles and sturdy grips to prevent accidental cuts. Because dermaplaning sloughs off dead skin cells, this hair removal tool provides the smoothest makeup application.

This method helps promote cell turnover and collagen production plus minimizes dark spots and uneven skin texture, dermatologist Azadeh Shirazi, MD, told WH. "When your skin is hairless and buildup-free, it looks super toned and bright," Dr. Shirazi said.

Since dermaplaning cuts the hairs at the top layer of skin, they'll grow back quicker than laser hair removal or waxing, says Dr. Murphy-Rose. So be ready to use this technique 1-4 times monthly, depending on how fast your hair regrows.

These creams typically contain ingredients like eflornithine, calcium hydroxide, calcium thioglycolate, sodium hydroxide, and sodium thioglycolate to dissolve keratin bond structures of strands, making them pain-free alternatives to tweezing, waxing, and sugaring. Let the cream sit for a few minutes, then wipe it away to reveal smoother skin. If your skin is super sensitive, you could leave it on for 1-3 minutes and then shave it off in warm water, dermatologist S. Manjula Jegasothy, MD, previously told us. Hair will begin to grow back within a few days, so you're looking at a low-key commitment.

These are best for removing hair from a small area of the face, like your eyebrows, chin, and upper lip. TBH, tweezing has mild to moderate discomfort since you're plucking hairs from the root. On a scale of 1—10, I'd rank the pain level from 4—6. Since I have sensitive eyes that easily water, I stay away from tweezing my brows, but I found plucking a rogue chin hair pretty mild. Upkeep depends on how fast your hair grows back. Some women want to pluck every few days, while others can stretch their tweezing schedule to every few weeks.

Dermaplaning tools and razors are either manual or electric powered by batteries or a USB or USB-C cable.

When you pick electric devices like the Dermaflash, you'll be spending around $200, while manual tools, including hair removal creams and razors are much cheaper. With prices ranging from $150 to $600, epilators and laser hair removal devices are definite investments, but still cost less than a series of professional sessions. Some of these splurge-worthy devices also have smart capabilities like Bluetooth, smartphone app features, and even calendar reminders to remove facial hair.

Ultimately, opt for the electric devices if you want a fast and powerful tool that lasts for years and don't mind the upfront cost. Choose a manual tool if you would rather save the cash for other beauty products, like an LED face mask or a red light therapy device for hair growth.

Keeping the above guidelines in mind—type, cost, and manual versus electric devices—I carefully researched dozens of at-home facial hair removal tools. After settling on 12 contenders, I spent several months testing and I was only truly impressed by a handful of picks. I evaluated their ease of use, how often I needed to remove hair, and how seamless they were to incorporate into my routine. Many at-home devices can’t distinguish between darker complexions and dark hair, dermatologist Dr. Nazarian previously told WH. Melanin-rich skin is at a higher risk of their skin absorbing laser hair and burning, said Dr. Nazarian. My advice: Either opt for a different alternative on this list or book an appointment with your dermatologist for specific guidance on the best laser hair removal treatment plan for you.

#6799. The 7 Best Electric Razors for Women in 2026, Tested by Beauty Editors

It's time to ditch the disposable before your holiday party.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

My interest in hair removal started in elementary school when I snuck into my mom's medicine cabinet and dragged her razor across my leg like I was coloring with my favorite box of markers. Needless to say, I've learned the right way to shave since then (namely to apply shaving cream and gently glide my razor toward the grain with light pressure).

I've had a lot of help perfecting my technique—as a beauty editor with a decade of testing experience, I have access to the best dermatologists and aestheticians. Yet, no matter how on-point my new shaving technique is now, most women's razors never get my legs quite as smooth. And other hair removal methods are super painful, ripping strands out at the root. Ouch!

But then, I upgraded to electric razors—and I've never turned back.

“A closer shave can feel nice, but because the surface of our skin is not perfectly smooth, it inevitably results in removal of some skin cells,” says dermatologist Caroline Robinson, MD. This can lead to microscopic nicks and irritation. “Electric razors can help avoid this. They’re typically less sensitizing,” says Dr. Robinson, because the shave they provide is not as close. At-home laser hair removal devices, waxing, sugaring, and threading are all great options for hair removal if you’ve got the time (and the coin), but if silky-smooth skin sans irritation is what you’re after, the answer is electric.

Over the past two years, Women's Health editors have been testing popular electric razors to determine which seven are worth the investment. Ultimately, we found that the Philips Beauty Series was the best multi-use product due to its five-blade razor and dual-sidedness that allows for a super close shave. With the multitude of options available on Amazon, Ulta, and Dermstore, there are a couple of features to consider the next time you're in the market for a fresh shave:

More is not always better. In fact, a larger number of blades can sometimes increase irritation, says dermatologist Lauren Ploch, MD. On the other hand, fewer blades mean more passes over the skin, which can also cause irritation. It's about finding a happy medium that works for you. This may take some testing, so remember to try out your razor on a small patch of skin to see how you react before going all in.

Think about how well a razor feels and fits in your hand. You want something that’s well-balanced and not too heavy for the most comfortable shaving experience. Also consider if it will shave wet or dry. Not all electric razors can be used in the shower, Dr. Ploch points out. So, if you're a shower shaver, make sure to grab something waterproof and cordless.

You also want to make sure your razor head can contour to your body's curves, especially when you're targeting areas like the bikini line, underarms, toes, and even legs. Some of the razors on this list, like the Panasonic Close Curves Electric Razor, can uniquely adapt to the body's curves with their rotating heads.

If a razor is rechargeable (big plus already), make sure to check how long it takes to charge and the battery life. If you don't want to deal with a cord or annoying charge time, some of the options on this list—hi, Philips Series 5000 Electric Facial Hair Remover—are battery-powered, which may be more convenient while traveling.

Some razors come with trimmers, blade guides, and changeable blade heads for different areas of the body. If you're looking for more of a Swiss Army knife-type, multi-tool situation, take a look at the available attachments. Then, see if those are compatible with the parts of your body you want to tackle.

Believe it or not, many razors have a warranty, so if you're unsatisfied with your purchase, you can most likely return it for a longer period than you might think. Most razors on this list have a 30-day warranty, which gives you ample time for testing.

For the past year, Women’s Health editors, including myself, and contributors Jasmine Gomez and Sabrina Talbert, tested the bestselling, most popular, and top-rated electric razors. Our team also consulted four dermatologists, and then evaluated thousands of customer reviews and ratings to narrow down the seven best electric razors for a wide range of needs and preferences.

Ready for smooth, bump-free skin? Look no further than these editor-tested and expert-approved electric razors for women, below.

If you struggle with bumps and irritation, an electric razor might be your new best friend. Of course, you still need to make sure you use that electric razor properly. Dermatologists say you should follow the below best practices for a smooth shave every time.

Make sure your skin is well hydrated before shaving because using an electric razor over dry, irritated skin can make matters worse, says dermatologist Joshua Zeichner, MD. Dr. Robinson adds that you should also use a gentle cleanser prior to shaving. And, avoid fragrance or products known to cause irritation immediately before and after shaving, she says.

And please, do so in the direction of the hair growth, says Dr. Zeichner. Repeated strokes over the same area increase the likelihood of irritation and ingrown hairs.

Hydration is key. "I advise patients that they do not need a dedicated post-shave or aftercare lotion—a gentle moisturizer is just fine in these areas," says Dr. Robinson, who likes light, fragrance-free formulas. Aside from that, Dr. Robinson recommends making sure you follow any specific instructions on the product package—in other words, you should actually read the directions.

At the end of the day, everyone's going to have their personal preference, and it's not worth, ahem, splitting hairs if you prefer one type of razor over another. That being said, if your skin is prone to irritation, it might be time to ditch the traditional razor. With an electric razor, you’re not scraping against the skin really hard like a traditional razor does, says dermatologist Cameron Rokhsar, MD. This means that you won't see a lot of the cuts and burns that sometimes result from a manual blade, says Dr. Rokhsar. There are also areas of the body that might be better suited to shave with an electric razor. "Most women's electric razors are designed for our curvy areas," says Dr. Ploch. "I prefer electric razors around the ankles because manual razors cut this area easily."

Keeping your electric razor clean is incredibly important, says Dr. Rokhsar. Many derms will recommend changing a manual razor every time you shave, but you can't do that with an electric one, which makes running the blades under warm water after use a crucial step. This will keep it free of hair, shaving cream, and other residues.

Dr. Rokhsar also recommends swiping over the blades and the outside of the razor with a rubbing alcohol-soaked cotton ball regularly. "Especially if it sits in your bathroom moisture, bacteria will accumulate on the blades," he says. "It's an easy entry point for bacteria to go down the hair follicle." That's a big no-no if you want to stay free of irritation or infection.

Our team of editors follow Dr. Rokhsar's advice and it hasn't led us astray. And despite evaluating some of these razors for close to a year, we are pleased that we haven't noticed any rusting after using the alcohol-soaked cotton ball.

Another tip is to actually read and follow the maintenance instructions of your device. (Admittedly, we threw ours away since we've been using these for several months and like to keep the limited space of our beauty closet as streamlined as possible.) You can also prioritize using devices like the Philips Series 5000 Electric Facial Hair Remover that's easy to clean, as the head can easily be removed so you can dump shaved hair and dead skin cells straight into the garbage.

#6778. This Unexpected French Antique Is Suddenly Making a Comeback, and You’ve Probably Seen It Before

The desire to preserve mementos from a wedding day transcends centuries and cultures. Today's brides still hang onto their wedding dresses, dry their bouquets, and spend a large portion of their budgets on photography. But in 19th- and 20th-century France, the art of preserving wedding-day memories took the form of globes de mariée, also known as marriage globes.

These ornate, shrine-like displays primarily showcased the bride's head crown and other keepsakes from the big day, and the additional pieces inside symbolized the couple's dreams for their life together. The cloche protected these special items from dust back then and still today.

Here's a closer look at the history and allure of globes de mariée, plus tips for sourcing and styling these gorgeous antiques.

Chris Featherston is the senior vice president of product acquisition at Austin Auction Gallery, and he and his family have been dealing in French antiques for more than 30 years. In the last couple of years alone, they've sold more than 150 globes de Mariée for an average of $400 to $600 each.

But this definitely wasn't always the case. Featherstone notes that the demand for these antiques has risen significantly in the past five years alone.

"In the early days of our business, most of what we sold came from house clearances in France," he recalls. "These globes were ubiquitous. At that time, we felt lucky to be able to sell them for $30 to $40 each."

Anyone with a strong appreciation for historic relics can see the appeal of these antiques. For instance, Mick and Paige Whitcomb have been collecting globes de mariée for nearly a decade, and they currently own 15. Their interest in these types of treasures is reflected in their careers. Mick creates sculptural lighting from architectural and industrial salvage dating back to the 1800s through his business, Artifacts by Nomad. Paige is a tintype photographer who captures staged scenes featuring antiques from the 1800s (including her globes de mariée) using a 19th-century large-format studio camera.

"While traveling in France for a wedding anniversary, we were first exposed to globes de mariée at the Marché aux Puces antique market in Paris," Mick says. "While we were initially caught by the beauty of the objects, after hearing their history and connection to French marriages, it felt like a beautiful way of commemorating our anniversary and trip to Europe."

Due to the highly custom nature of globes de marriée, each one is a unique find. However, Featherston notes that most contain a few similar components. For starters, the bride's headpiece often rests on a red cushion, and it typically shows off delicate beeswax orange blossoms that symbolize purity and virginity.

Most also contain small mirrors, which carried different meanings depending on the shape. For example, diamond-shaped mirrors symbolize how many children the couple wished to have, and rectangular mirrors stood for the length of time the couple knew each other before their engagement.

You'll also often find a wreath embellished with brass ornaments, usually flowers, birds and tree leaves. These symbolize love, a long-lasting marriage, and happiness.

"The globes de marriées integrate beautiful symbolism in every piece, and I find them fascinating," Paige says. "Each globe is so distinctive, and tells the story of the couple that created it. You can surmise the couple's hopes and dreams by minutiae tucked away behind the dome for safekeeping. I find very intimate details incorporated into the displays, such as a prayer card of a favorite saint, to photos of the bride and groom. I have even found hair and teeth, transforming the antique into a memento mori as well."

Mick has noticed that sourcing these antiques is considerably more difficult in the United States than in Europe. But with a little patience and determination, it is possible.

"Because of the glass domes, I think fewer importers are brave enough to bring them over," he says. "Despite this, we have found the occasional gem in the wild." In the States, he's had the most luck sourcing them from design-forward antique dealers, online auction sites and even prop houses that have just wrapped up a period film project.

Since there is a constant demand for these pieces, Featherston tries to feature several in every sale at Austin Auction Gallery.

Globes de mariée can add a formal and traditional flair to any space, and they even mix well with more modern accents.

"One of my favorite ways to showcase the globes de mariée is to mix them with a piece of contemporary art," Paige says. "I love to create a harmony between our collections of antiques and artwork from artists that are living and working today."

For example, Mick and Paige have styled two of their favorite globes de mariée with the piece "The Gardener" by working artist L.J. Eidolon. "I find collecting modern works that have a historic feeling to them helps create balance between the multiple centuries displayed together in one scene," Paige says.
