#6776. The 10 Best Cross-Training Shoes for Women, Tested by Editors

We put dozens of shoes through lifts, sprints, and lateral moves to find the most stable, versatile picks for every kind of gym workout.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

OUR FITNESS EDITORS love to slip on cushy sneakers like the Hoka Clifton 11 for our 5Ks and hot girl walks. But on days when we're lifting heavy, doing a functional strength training workout, or have a gym sesh that switches between lifts and sprints, we turn to our favorite cross-training shoes. If you’re unfamiliar with the term, cross-training is combining different exercise modalities—like cardio, resistance training, and plyometric (rapid movements)—during a gym session. Think of these sneakers as multipurpose powerhouses. They keep your feet locked in, whether you’re deadlifting or speed walking on an under-desk treadmill.

“They’re designed to handle side-to-side movement, quick pivots, and changes in direction—not just forward motion,” says Melissa Lockwood, DPM, a podiatrist at Heartland Foot and Ankle Associates in Bloomington, Illinois. “This makes them more supportive for gym workouts, classes, or sports where your foot needs stability in multiple planes, like pickleball or tennis.” On the flip side, a walking or running shoe usually only supports you in straight-line motion, Lockwood says.

Wearing running sneakers for strength training workouts is generally a no-no (except for the caveat about the Brooks Adrenaline GTS 25 below). All the podiatrists I spoke with say it’s safer and beneficial to invest in cross-training shoes. That’s because they usually have flatter outsoles and thinner midsoles than running shoes to keep your feet locked in, says Richard H. Graves, DPM, a podiatrist at Sol Foot & Ankle Centers in Long Beach, California. That way, you won’t wobble during your deadlifts and box jumps. The material of cross-training shoes is also more durable than running shoes to resist wear and tear, and has thick heel counters to help prevent ankle injuries.

Whether you're a seasoned weightlifting junkie or a runner on a mission to integrate more training modalities into your routine, read on for the best cross-training shoes you can find.

After testing dozens of cross-training shoes this past year, the latest lululemon Chargefeel 3 stands out for its impeccable support and stability during speedy, lateral movements like ice skaters, kettlebell squats, and Zumba workouts. We’ve been fans of the Chargefeel line for years, with the previous edition—the Chargefeel 2—winning the best cross-training shoe for our 2025 WH Sneaker Awards. Lululemon has since made a few tweaks that make it even better. “Despite the fact that sneaker fit and feel is extremely subjective, everyone I know who has worked out in the Chargefeel—myself included—has loved its perfect balance of stability and flexibility,” says WH executive health and fitness director Jacqueline Andriakos, NASM-CPT.

It’s now 8 ounces instead of 9.1 ounces, and has a lower heel-to-toe drop—6 mm compared to 9.5 mm—to help alleviate stress on the ankles and feet. WH associate style and commerce editor Shannen Zitz loves the wider build for lifting and squatting. She says her feet feel locked in and extra secure. “My feet stay securely in place thanks to a forefoot-hugging band on the inside of the shoe and a tough plastic heel counter,” says Zitz. She also notes that the springy EVA foam cushioning makes it a workhorse for walking and running.

And post-workout, it’s a great leisure kick for traveling or running errands. “I recently wore these while traveling, and they kept my feet super happy power walking through the airport and standing on long security and customs lines,” Zitz adds. Andriakos agrees, stating, “The stylish silhouette looks good outside the gym.”

The fit is true to size, and the breathable knit upper doesn’t feel constricting when wearing thick socks (perfect for combating blisters!). Although wide size options are not available, the toes still have adequate room to spread with each step or lift. However, Zitz notes the wide outsole may feel too bulky during quick and dynamic HIIT workouts.

We wouldn’t recommend most running shoes for cross-training, but the Brooks Adrenaline GTS 25 is an exception. You can effortlessly transition from long runs to deadlifts, thanks to its balanced cushioning and moderate heel-to-toe drop.

Compared to the GTS 24, the latest version has slightly softer cushioning, with about 3 mm more foam in the forefoot and 1 mm in the heel, giving it a smoother, more comfortable feel underfoot. It also drops from 12 mm to 10 mm, creating a slightly more level feel. That can make it feel more stable during strength work than higher-drop running shoes.

At 9 ounces, it’s a lightweight gem, especially for those with overpronation or flat feet during agility drills. Its GuideRails technology—firm foam on both sides of the foot—keeps feet aligned during everything from Bulgarian split squats to lunges to speed walking. Plus, stiff heel padding keeps feet from sliding."These feel super comfortable and supportive for everyday life, traditional workouts, walks, easy runs—you name it," says Melissa Kendter, CPT, a functional strength coach. "I love that this shoe feels well cushioned but doesn't have a huge chunky sole, making it more appropriate for strength workouts than most running shoes."

Because the outsole is relatively smooth (it’s built for road miles, not side-to-side gym moves), we’d stick to non-slip floors for quick lateral work. If your workouts are heavy on lifting or plyometrics, a flatter cross-trainer like the Nike Metcon 10 may feel steadier and more secure underfoot.

Read More: Brooks Adrenaline GTS 25 Review

We’ve been fans of the Nano series since 2018, and every year we notice Reebok levels up its cushioning and heel stability to keep our feet locked in and comfortable during even the most intense workouts. The midsole padding is firm for balancing during heavy lifts and lateral movements, yet is still bouncy for excellent rebound during HIIT workouts and explosive jumps. The mesh material upper is also firm, keeping you steady when lifting heavy.

MH digital designer Taryn Colbert used to prefer flat lifestyle shoes like Converse or Vans, but has since switched over to the Nano X5 for her cross-training sessions. The stiffer soles offer her better balance, while she notes that the heel counter is dense, meaning it doesn’t flex easily. However, the 7 mm heel-to-toe drop may be too steep for some weightlifters who prefer a ground-feel shoe. But it’s a pro if you like extra heel support.

The grippy outsole delivers superior traction when executing deadlifts or weighted squats, while the snug laces never budge. Colbert also notes that the breathable material helps combat sweaty feet. Just note that the fit runs tight (consider ordering at least a half-size up), and it weighs 12.1 ounces, making it one of the heaviest shoes in this guide.

If your workouts focus on dynamic moves like kettlebell swings or heavy lifting, the Nobull Drive is a gem. It’s crafted with a breathable mesh knit upper that feels sock-like and keeps feet cool during grueling reps.

The balance of flexibility and firmness makes it simple to transition from the squat rack to the HIIT studio. WH associate editor Olivia Luppino wears them during physical therapy to help maintain balance when doing Bosu ball squats, and then hits the gym to do deadlifts. She also notes the mesh isn’t scratchy—an issue she has had when wearing this material.

The subtle foam feels similar to a minimally-cushioned running shoe, while the low 4 mm drop keeps Luppino grounded when deadlifting. She also notes that a firm heel counter makes her feet feel secure without too much stiffness. And at 8.3 ounces, it’s the lightest shoe in this guide, which makes it an optimal choice for sprints and mixed-modality workouts.

Nike’s latest Metcon 10 is a top pick among our editors when it comes to pumping iron. Its main update compared to the last iteration is a smaller, stiffer plate strategically placed under the heel to help stabilize the feet when squatting heavy reps or doing deadlifts. It’s also lighter at 11.8 ounces (it was previously 13.3 ounces) and feels more responsive during jumps and HIIT when it’s not leg day.

Zitz was completely sold after her first wear. “I feel totally grounded with the 4 mm drop while squatting and lunging, and they never weigh me down while running,” says Zitz. “I also love how sleek and stylish they look (especially in the pink color!), and I've been wearing them with nearly every athleisure fit since I got them.”

The tough rubber traction is another highlight. It feels almost sticky, helping you stay put. Zitz adds that the grooves on the outsole are great for slick sidewalks or dynamic movements, like box jumps. The mesh upper also offers some breathability, but it’s not the most airy shoe in this guide.

The RAD One V2 is so flexible that it feels like an extension of your feet during lifts, drills, and cardio. The forefoot bends easily and delivers bounce that feels natural. Accardi says her movement feels “controlled, yet free” when these are on her feet. “While firmness is important in a cross-training shoe, many are too stiff, which limits power in workouts unrelated to lifting,” she says. “These feel nimble yet stable for agility drills and plyometric moves like box jumps.”

The moderate 6 mm drop and heel counter provide balance and sturdiness. The springy EVA midsole also alleviates joint impact during high-impact moves. And bonus: the cushioning is wrapped with rubber to help combat wear and tear, while the textured outsole provides perfect traction during lateral bounds and tuck jumps. “My footing feels nice and steady during transitions, whether I’m going fast or slow.”

Note that this kick is the heaviest option in this guide, weighing 12.8 ounces. It’s not the best option for long-distance runs, but it’s a top pick if you’re all about explosive movements.

On is a cult-fave among runners for its cloud-like outsole that’s become a style statement, but the Cloudpulse Pro proves the brand is also a game changer in cross-training shoes. Besides short sprints, it’s designed for workouts that blend cardio and strength—box jumps, sprints, step-ups, and ice skaters. Its signature CloudTec cushioning—compressive foam that absorbs impact—is ultra-bouncy and makes you feel like you’re flying during jumps and agility drills. It propels you forward whether you’re walking, running, or doing dynamic movements. One word that describes it perfectly is energetic. The foam also isn’t overly soft, which is great for support during fast transitions.

The 6 mm heel-to-toe drop keeps Accardi grounded and balanced during lateral and dynamic movements. A firm heel counter keeps her feet secure, which gives her the confidence to grind hard. Weighing 10.6 ounces, it feels resilient yet solid. The thick, grippy outsole also helps her maintain control on smooth, slick floors. Plus, the polyester material evaporates sweat to keep feet dry.

The fit is roomy, especially in the toe box, making it an excellent option for those with wide feet or bunions. “The fit is perfection,” says WH food producer Tina Martinez. “It’s streamlined through the narrowest part of my foot and hugs my arches in the most secure way.”

If you want a marshmallow-like feel in your cross-training kicks, the While On Earth Move Trainer is a star pick that’s plush minus the bulk. The spongy EVA foam is ideal for short or long runs, while a heel counter (hard plastic that wraps around the heel) prevents any budging during weight training. It’s also light and nimble at 10.6 ounces, making it a workhorse for cardio and strength work.

WH deputy visual editor Dangi McCoy says it’s super supportive during at-home workouts and lateral movements like lunges and shuffling. Plus, it requires no break-in period before hitting the gym. “The width of the shoe itself is great for stability—my feet and toes are secure with any movement,” says McCoy. The wide base and toe box offer ample space for natural foot movement, especially during fast circuit training and intervals, while the neutral 6 mm drop promotes even landings.

Another nice feature: It’s stain and water-resistant. While this shoe is comfortable enough for running, the Brooks Adrenaline GTS 24 is better for long runs. But sprints and short-distance running workouts? For sure.

Weekly cross-training puts wear and tear on your shoes, so picking a durable pair is a priority. The GORUCK Ballistic Trainer is strong and resilient enough to handle demanding training from plyometrics to climbing. The Cordura nylon upper is abrasion-resistant and strong, while the rubber outsole is thick and sturdy to sustain repetitive force. Accardi has even worn it to rock climb. “The fabric still looks like it did straight out of the box after scraping against a climbing wall,” says Accardi. “The sole also kept me from slipping on challenging areas.”

The 8 mm drop feels level, making it practical for barbell squats or deadlifts, while the EVA foam midsole is plush enough to absorb impact during sprints and jumps. And don’t worry—it doesn’t skimp on stability. A heel counter secures the feet to the insole during weight-bearing exercises. As for fit, a wide toe box allows for comfy movement.

Nike took the best traits of its iconic Free running shoe to create the Metcon 6—a trusty hybrid running and cross trainer. Whether you’re executing agility drills, speed work, or lunges, it delivers the perfect combination of comfort and stability, no matter what workout you have on deck. "This is perfect for my training workouts that consist of part speed run intervals and part strength training because of its combination of cushioning and stability," says Ashley Barnett, NASM-CPT. She adds that it’s great for jumping, strength routines, and mixed workouts. Accardi also loves wearing it during HIIT classes, highlighting its flexibility and responsiveness when transitioning from burpees to high knees.

Compared to the Metcon 10, it has a higher drop at 7.2 mm. While this is a pro in a running shoe, it may mean having less stability during heavy lifts. It’s also worth noting that it fits snug and narrow, so you may have to go up a half size. Accardi has bunions and had to size up to prevent the fabric from rubbing against the sides of her feet during runs and agility drills.

Everyone has a unique foot shape, and each brand can fit differently. So, finding the right fit can be complex. But as a general rule of thumb, your feet should feel supported, not suffocated, and you shouldn't be counting down the minutes until you can kick them off, says Mali Pelham, CPT, C.F.S.C., a personal trainer at Wattage in Chicago.

An easy way to measure if you have enough space is by being able to slide your finger back and forth at the back of the heel. Also, your shoes shouldn’t feel like they’re pinching the middle of your foot—this can lead to tingling and numbness if they’re too tight. If you’re completely unsure, it doesn’t hurt to get fitted at a shoe store. Referencing brands’ sizing charts can also be helpful. Also, pay attention to width, as different brands offer narrow and wide options.

Heel-to-toe drop refers to the height difference between the heel and the forefoot of the shoe.

Consider the type of workouts you'll be tackling in your new kicks. Each demands specific qualities in a shoe, though the best cross-training shoes for women are versatile enough to let you do all these workouts in them.

The Women’s Health team has spent almost seven years (since 2018!) testing and curating the ultimate guide to the best cross-training shoes for women by putting more than 50 cross-training shoes through their paces. Our rigorous testing process involved multiple phases, including scouring reviews, consulting industry experts, and most importantly, testing the shoes firsthand. We laced up cross-trainers for a battery of tests designed to assess performance across various activities, including weightlifting, cardio, agility drills, and CrossFit workouts. Then, we evaluated whether or not a shoe is functional for different kinds of workouts, has enough stability to prevent ankle rolling, is durable enough to withstand wear and tear, and is comfortable for long hours at the gym. The guide below includes the shoes we were most impressed by.

#6771. 7 Best Weighted Blankets to Sleep Better and Ease Anxiety, According to Editors

Shop our favorites from Bearaby, Layla, Gravity, and more.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Weighted blankets are a force in the sleep world, with fans swearing they can do everything from easing anxiety to helping you sleep better. While there are some small studies to support those claims, the science is still catching up.

Still, if you’ve spent any amount of time wrapped in a weighted blanket, you know how cozy it can feel. You’re basically in a cocoon, protected from the outside world. That extra weight also means this blanket won’t just slide off during the night, keeping you nice and snug all night long.

How do they work, you ask? The concept is pretty simple: These types of blankets mimic healing touch—something that reduces cortisol (the stress hormone) and boosts oxytocin (the feel-good hormone), Heidi Hanna, PhD, author of Stressaholic and an advisory board member for the American Institute of Stress, explains.

“While there isn’t much research to verify, the theory is that a weighted blanket works much like a hug,” adds Leisha Cuddihy, PhD, a clinical psychologist in behavioral sleep medicine. Hugging lowers blood pressure and stress hormones, which she says can help you snooze soundly. So, if you’re looking for some much-needed relief in the p.m., a weighted blanket is worth a try. Weighted blankets also make excellent holiday presents—who doesn’t want the gift of sleep?

Not all weighted blankets are created equal, though. Fill, material, and the actual weight of the blanket matter.

The most Instagrammable beauty of the bunch, Bearaby’s chunky weighted blanket is hand-knitted with layers of responsibly farmed organic cotton. Plus, since it doesn’t have fillers that pack in warmth, you’ll stay cozy without melting into a sweaty puddle overnight.

WH social media editor Alyssa Cruz slept with this blanket for a month and says it feels “just right” at night. “I absolutely love this blanket,” she shares, adding that the Bearaby blanket has improved her quality of sleep. “It is extremely comforting, and I find myself falling asleep within minutes,” she says. The blanket is so popular that Cruz and her roommate have taken turns sleeping with it. “The only cons is the size,” Cruz says. “You definitely have to be strategic with how you place it on your body, because it is very rectangular.” (Bearaby offers three different weights and sizes, giving you options to work with.) But Cruz says she’s still hooked. “I love it, and would buy ten more if I could.”

Make way for the OG. This weighted blanket made its debut in 2017, following the makers’ successful $5 million Kickstarter campaign to fund production. Gridded stitching on the blanket ensures that glass beads are distributed evenly, reaching every pressure point. Plus, we love the velvety flannel material for colder months.

Gravity has a smart design that makes this blanket easy to care for: Just remove the outside cover and toss it in the washing machine. (The inner blanket is hand wash only.) If you tend to run hot, this isn’t the blanket for you—it can get pretty toasty at night. But if you’re on the hunt for a snuggly weighted blanket that’s convenient and comfortable, give this one a go.

Weighted blankets tend to be pricey, with some costing upwards of $200. But the ZZZhen weighted blanket is just $30, making it accessible to most people. The blanket has a smooth exterior and internal grid system that’s designed to keep the interior glass beads in place.

This blanket does the trick, delivering that classic warm hug feeling people crave in a weighted blanket. It’s also soft, and machine washable, making it easy to care for. We like that it comes in a 7-pound size, giving people new to weighted blankets a lighter-weight option if they want to ease in.

Of course, there are a few sacrifices to consider while you’re saving money. It’s unclear what the blanket is made from—some descriptions say cotton, while others say polyester. The sizing also tends to run a little small. But you can still get a lot for $30 with this option.

Weighted blankets take a little more thought than regular blankets. You have to figure out a weight that’s best for your needs, along with choosing a color that fits your decor. While many weighted blanket companies limit you to two or three weight options, YnM allows you to pick from 11 choices, along with 22 shades, giving you plenty of room for customization.

The blanket is made from a lightweight cotton for a surprisingly breezy feel. Available in weights from 7 to 50 pounds, the blanket is composed of seven layers that snugly envelop your every curve. But its largest size is 88” by 104”, allowing you to share it with a partner.

Available in three, eight, and 10-pound options, the Lola Weighted Blanket is great for anyone who wants some heft without feeling too heavy. The double-sided OEKO-TEX Standard 100-certified faux fur is unbelievably silky-soft, plus Its stain-resistant and sheds zero.

“It kept me warm and cozy without being overwhelming because of the extra weight,” says WH social media producer Rianna Cafaro. “It was just right.” While some weighted blankets look utilitarian, that’s not the case with the Lola blanket. It’s fuzzy exterior makes it incredibly inviting. While Cafaro didn’t notice any drawbacks of this blanket during testing, she did point out that it’s pricey. Still, she says, “it is worth the price.”

Straying from the pack, Layla’s blanket is available in only one weight, depending on your mattress size. But that means it’s enough for you and your bedmate to share (score!). The top layer is plush, while the other feels like a cool cotton bedsheet.

WH fitness and reviews editor Natascha Grief loved the fabric options, especially the plush side. “It kind of feels like being hugged by a stuffed animal,” she says. Grief says that the blanket delivers a comforting feel without the bulk. “The fill was silent and virtually unnoticeable,” she says. “It didn't shift around every time I did.” Grief also says she didn’t notice any uncomfortable pressure points like she has in the past as a side sleeper with other weighted blankets.

The only downside, per Grief, is that this only comes in one color (black). While you can toss the whole thing in the wash, just be aware of your machine’s capacity. Larger sizes may be tough to stuff into a compact washer.

There’s a wide range of textures with weighted blankets—some have a stuffed animal-like feel, while others are more functional. The Baloo weighted blanket falls into the second camp, with a comforter style design you can toss on your bed and leave there.

WH entertainment editor Amy Wilkinson praises the temperature regulation of this blanket, noting that it felt just right. It’s on the thinner side, but is great for layering and all-season use, she points out—just add on another layer in the winter and take one off in the summer. “The 15-pound weight felt just right for getting the soothing compression of a weighted blanket without feeling like you’re being squished in your sleep,” Wilkinson says. Just be aware that you may need to size up. Wilkinson got a full/queen size and found that it didn’t cover her entire queen bed. Still, she says, “as a no-frills comforter, it does the job.”

It’s no surprise that this is the most important consideration. There’s no one perfect weight. Rather, it’s essential to get a blanket that’s right for your body type. Weighted blankets typically range from 5 to 30 pounds, and many experts suggest picking one that’s around 10 percent of your body weight. This will ensure you don’t experience any discomfort as you sleep. Ideally, you want the right amount of pressure without putting so much weight on your body that you feel “trapped” or suffocated.

You’ll want a blanket that offers uniform pressure to feel the calming effect, says Cuddihy. Look for quality stitching, layers of material, and glass-bead fill. Some lower-quality weighted blankets will have plastic pellets instead of glass beads, so keep an eye out when shopping.

Depending on the time of year and whether you run hot or cold, the material your weighted blanket is made from matters. If you’re a hot sleeper, look for blankets made with breathable fabrics like bamboo or cotton. A removable cover can be helpful, too, for easier machine washing. If you like to be bundled in warmth under your blankets, look for materials like fleece or microfiber.

Weighted blankets need to be washed, too, making it important to find one that won’t make your life harder. Blankets filled with glass microbeads, plastic pellets, steel beads, or pebbles can usually go in the wash, while those that are filled with organic materials often can’t. Your washing machine may also not be able to handle the extra weight. So, read the care instructions before purchasing your blanket to make sure it lines up with your lifestyle.

There’s a lot of variety with weighted blanket sizing, making it important to read the measurements carefully. If you plan to use your weighted blanket on your bed like a comforter, or just want to make sure it’ll cover your body while you snooze, try measuring out the size on your bed to see what it will look like to get a better sense. Consider sizing up if you’re unsure.

Our editors regularly test bedding, including weighted blankets, and they continue to try new products as they’re released. We last updated this story in March. Since then, we tested four additional weighted blankets for a month or more.

We spent many nights at home snuggled under weighted blankets comparing weight, texture, temperature regulation, size, and performance. Naturally, comfort was heavily weighed during testing. When testing, we also took into consideration which blankets were best for the sofa versus for actually sleeping under for the night.

#6768. 7 Best At-Home Waxing Kits, Tested by Beauty Experts

Of the 25 wax kits we tested, Flamingo was our favorite.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Body hair is completely normal and natural. But if you’re like me and prefer a hair-free look, then take a page from my beauty editor routine and try at-home waxing kits for dolphin-like skin. Dermatologists told me that at-home waxing kits are a worthwhile hair removal method. Unlike shaving, waxing removes hair at the root, whereas a woman's razor blade simply cuts the hair down, says dermatologist Reshmi Kapoor, MD. It’s a bit painful, but the tenderness quickly disappears and the results are so worth the temporary discomfort. Waxing may lead to finer, sparser regrowth over time, so the skin stays hair-free longer than with shaving, says Dr. Kapoor. My underarms are dramatically smoother, lighter, and have fewer ingrown hairs to boot.

At-home waxing kits prevent unsightly razor bumps and are more cost-effective than replacing dull razors and restocking shaving creams. They're also cheaper than professional waxing sessions and save you time from commuting to the waxing studio.

And sure, applying a glob of wax to your body sounds intimidating, but it's surprisingly easy and not as painful as you'd imagine. In our guide to pain-free waxing, experts revealed some helpful advice, like staying hydrated with water and avoiding alcohol before waxing. Another pro tip: skip waxing a few days before your period because you’re dealing with increased sensitivity. Plus, some at-home waxing kits even contain soothing ingredients like aloe vera that are safe for sensitive skin.

With a low-upkeep routine of every four to six weeks, using at-home waxing kits is a no-brainer beauty move. Ahead, Dr. Kapoor and her fellow dermatologists get real about shopping for at-home waxing kits and share their must-have picks. After ordering our editor-tested, dermatologist-approved recommendations, keep reading for more expert tips on post-waxing care.

Out of all the different at-home waxing kits we tried, these wax strips were the easiest to use and the most effective at picking up every single little hair. Flamingo's wax strips won our top spot because they were super simple to apply with very little prep work besides getting the area clean and dry. The soft wax strips are infused with soothing ingredients, like castor seed oil and beeswax, making them ideal for removing fine hair on larger areas, like your arms. Best of all, they’re ready to go whenever you are—no microwave is required. To use them, simply peel apart the strip and apply it, rubbing firmly against your skin to make sure it coats every hair.

We did find the strips a little too big for areas like the face, but you can always cut them down to the correct size with a pair of scissors. Pulling up the wax strip stung quite a bit for Montemayor, but it was over within seconds and the wax effortlessly removed most of her hair on the first go. The strips are very sticky, so she reapplied it to the same area to ensure every last hair was yanked. She especially likes that the post-wax cloth feels very soothing on the skin, and that it helps remove any excess product and stickiness. Montemayor says her redness went away after about an hour, and she was hair-free for a good two weeks after that.

Hard wax kits tend to be messy when they’re applied or removed incorrectly. Remember to always apply a thin, even layer and pull the wax in the opposite direction of hair growth for a seamless waxing experience. However, the results are similar to those you would get at a waxing salon. This Sliick kit comes with a silicone cup and spatula, which makes the heating and application process a breeze. It takes a few rounds of heating and stirring the hard wax to get the right consistency. Once it starts looking like caramel, you’re ready to wax—and the waiting window is totally worth it because the hard wax is easy to maneuver and contains antioxidant-rich acai berry.

“Use a new stick each time you dip into the wax pot to avoid introducing bacteria, which could potentially cause infections,” says Gail Strong, an esthetician and CEO at Invigorate Spa in Indianapolis, Indiana.

The wax itself was easy to spread, but we found that it works best when laid in a thick layer, as thick as a banana peel. Montemayor likes that letting the wax cool completely before pulling the strip gives her a better result. The wax strip came up in one piece, lifting and removing all the hairs in its path. Unlike some of the hard wax kits we tried, this one didn’t leave any wax crumbs behind. Once you’re done waxing, all you have to do is let the wax in the cup and on the applicator dry, and it easily peels off. Montemayor found that after waxing her legs with this kit, it took about 10 days before she noticed the hair growing back.

Pro tip: Remember to dip a fresh stick into the wax pot to avoid introducing bacteria that can cause infections, says Strong.

Waxing tends to irritate the skin, but these strips from Billie were the most gentle of all the wax strips we tried. The sticky formula contains added castor oil, avocado oil, and aloe vera to soothe the skin, while the no-rub soft-gel wax attaches to every hair, whether it’s very fine or extra coarse.I used the Billie strips on my legs and underarms, and it was the least painful at-home waxing kit I tried. It’s a little tricky getting all of the hairs of my underarms, but the strips are very sticky, so I can go over the area two or three times and I’m left with smooth, hair-free pits. These strips didn’t leave behind wax on my skin, so cleanup is easy. I’ll just follow up with the included post-wax serum and my redness and minimal irritation are gone.The medium-sized wax strips are great for larger areas like the underarms and legs, but the small strips are too big to use on the upper lip or around the eyebrows. An easy hack is to trim them to fit your face—and with 32 wax strips in total, the $9 kit remains a great value.

Sometimes you just want a product that works for thousands of people. This all-inclusive kit earned a 4.4-star average from more than 38,350 Amazon reviews. More than 26,460 of them were perfect 5-star ratings. Casual.

So what makes it so impressive among Amazon shoppers? This kit includes quite literally everything you could need for a seamless at-home waxing experience. Waxing beads, an adjustable heat warmer, and 20 sticks for sanitary application? Of course. Skin prep spray and a soothing aftercare spray? Definitely. An instructional booklet with access to advice from a waxing expert via a smartphone app? Yup. It’s like the salon is truly in your bathroom.

Honestly, the biggest con about this device is that it requires some work. You’ve got to plug in the digital wax melt warmer and set the temperature; insert the wax bean bag into the removable waxing pot, and clean up the warmer and spatulas after. But TBH, based on the glowing Amazon reviews it’s a relatively small tradeoff worth the upkeep.

Women with sensitive skin or a low pain tolerance will love this sugaring wax because it’s gentle on the skin without compromising on hair removal. Its screw-on, roll-on applicator makes applying this wax a cinch, allowing you to reach tricky spots, like your chin or underarm, more precisely. You can easily wash the applicator after each use with soap and water. And to nourish the skin, the formula contains 100 percent organic virgin argan oil from Morocco.

Considering the above, it's no surprise that WH contributors love this buy, too. Its compact size is excellent for touch-ups on the go. And it’s a fantastic mess-free alternative that’s easy to apply and smells delicious, says Jackson. Plus, the wax works on shorter hairs as small as two millimeters, so it removes prickly stubble. While she fully recommends this at-home waxing kit, it’s worth noting that it’s not a hard wax, and it comes with cloth strips that may not cling to thicker hair types.

These Sally Hansen wax strips are infused with vitamin E and avocado oil for a more nourishing and less irritating waxing experience. The full-size strips are still large enough to cover a good amount of ground—like armpits and legs—and can leave you hair-free for up to eight weeks. You can also snip these strips to fit different areas of your face and body, including your underarms and bikini line.

Before Jackson switched over to laser hair removal, she always kept these strips on hand all the time. She used them for years and suggests putting a dish towel in the microwave for 20 seconds and placing the strip in the towel to warm it up before using it for better results. You can also follow up with a separate oil or serum, since it occasionally leaves a sticky residue on the skin that can be uncomfortable.

These waxing sticks are different from anything else we tried. Despite looking like something you would find in a kid's craft box, these don't play around when it comes to their ability to remove facial hair. The kit comes with two crayon-looking wax sticks, one small and one large. The small one is great for getting the errant hairs around your brows, while the larger stick is the perfect size for removing upper lip hair and peach fuzz on the rest of the face. Montemayor found that the stick made her feel like she had more control over where the wax went, which is especially important in sensitive areas like the face.To heat up the wax, all you need is a hair dryer. We recommend using the wax sticks in a bathroom because you’ll need to reheat the sticks often. The sticks are a little stringy, and the application can get messy because you’re trying to catch the wax and apply it before it continues dripping down the stick.

The hard wax has a pliable texture that snugly fits the curves of your face and can be applied in a nice, thick layer, which makes it easier to remove. We could do two to three swipes with the stick before we needed to blast it with the hair dryer again. The wax formula itself was very gentle and didn’t leave any sticky residue behind or tug at the top layer of skin. Overall, it was mostly painless because you’re working in such small sections. Once the stick dries, you can tuck it away for safekeeping and reuse it again the next time you need to remove your facial hair. We particularly like these sticks because they're travel-friendly, too. You can definitely throw them in a toiletry bag on your next weekend getaway.

With only three key factors to keep in mind, shopping for at-home waxing kits is refreshingly simple.

Before waxing, you should know there are two types of waxes: hard and soft.

If you want to use something stripless, go for hard wax. It adheres to the hair follicle (versus the skin), hardens, and can be removed on its own, eliminating the need for paper strips. For some, it may even feel less painful than soft wax, so it's great for sensitive skin, says dermatologist Nazanin Saedi, MD. The only caveat is that you may have to apply it several times in the same area to make sure all the hairs you target are removed.

Soft wax, on the other hand, is soft and applied in a thin layer. A paper strip is then used to adhere and remove the wax. Often, the top layer of skin is also removed, so it can be irritating for people with sensitive skin.

Ideally, beginners should look for kits that contain everything they need to get the job done—wax, strips, and finishing oil, at minimum. Novices may also appreciate waxing kits that come with pre-loaded strips, since they're less messy and require less precision. The tradeoff? You're limited to the size of the included strips, so look for kits with multiple sizes if you're targeting different areas of the body. Larger strips are fantastic for your legs, while small strips are practical for your face.

It's important to use the safest and gentlest wax kits available to avoid irritating, burning, and potentially scarring your skin. If you have sensitive skin, look for waxing kits that include skin-soothing ingredients, like castor and almond oils or aloe. And whatever you use, first test the wax in an area on your body that isn't too visible. This will allow you to see if it is gentle enough for your skin. If you experience a bad reaction, you'll know it's time to pivot to a different product.

For the past year, Women’s Health contributors and I, including Sabrina Talbert, Jasmine Gomez, Danielle Jackson, and Cristina Montemayor, tested the bestselling, most popular, and top-rated at-home waxing kits. Our team consulted dermatologists Blair Murphy-Rose, MD, Nazanin Saedi, MD, and Uchenna Okereke, MD, for pre-waxing advice and aftercare instructions to prevent irritation and promote healthy skin. Then, we got our hands on the kits and tested them in the comfort of our own homes, evaluating attributes like ease of use, comfort, results, and overall value. Of the 25 at-home waxing kits tested, the seven on this list delivered the best results in our in-depth evaluations.

At-home waxing removes body hair conveniently and cost-effectively, and the best kits do it quickly while minimizing pain and irritation. So, if you’re ready to ditch the razors, check out this list of the best waxing kits, tested by dermatologists and WH editors.

After waxing, you can prevent ingrown hairs from forming and minimize irritation immediately afterward by applying cool aloe to soothe skin, and sticking to loose clothes that won't create friction. "Aloe vera has anti-inflammatory properties that help soothe sunburned skin, while glycerin specifically can help to boost hydration levels to restore the skin's moisture barrier," dermatologist Divya Shokeen, MD, previously told WH in our guide to the best aloe vera gel. Some kits also come with nourishing oils and wipes to help minimize any post-wax irritation.

Before you head off to wax unwanted hair, these are a few other tips you should keep in mind.

Waxing removes hair by lifting it out at the root. With traditional waxing, hot wax is applied to skin and the hair is pulled off in the opposite direction of the way it's growing. The key word here and what can make at-home waxing dangerous is that it's hot.

“Hot wax can burn the skin—and it is a particularly dangerous type of burn because the hot product adheres to the skin,” says Dr. Murphy-Rose. Always make sure you're following the heating directions closely and that you hold your hand over the wax after it's been heated to gauge the temperature before applying it to your skin.

Then, first test the wax in an area on your body that isn't too visible. "That way you can check the temperature, but also see how your skin reacts," says Dr. Saedi. Testing a product will also allow you to see if it is gentle enough for your skin. “It's important to use the safest and gentlest wax kits available to avoid irritating, burning, and potentially scarring your skin," says Dr. Murphy-Rose. You should also avoid any ingredients you have a known allergy or sensitivity to. If you experience a bad reaction, you'll know it's time to pivot to a different product.

"Hot wax tends to adhere the strongest, so while it may remove hairs more efficiently, it can also stick to skin more and pull off those top layers of the skin as it's also pulling out your hair,” says Dr. Murphy-Rose.

Waxing exfoliates the top layer of skin and is more likely to cause hyperpigmentation a.k.a. dark spots, says Dr. Okereke. She says to think of the skin after waxing like a fresh, pink wound. "Ultraviolet rays are attracted to wounds, making the freshly waxed skin more susceptible to darkening as melanin (the dark pigment in skin) floods the area to try and protect it." Luckily, there is a simple fix: Wear broad-spectrum sunscreen! Better yet, keep that new skin out of the sun completely for at least a few days.

You'll also want to avoid any extra exfoliation, like retinol, chemical peels, and microdermabrasion, a few days prior and a few days following your at-home wax. A few other good things to know before getting started: Always wax on clean skin to avoid bacterial contamination in pores and make sure your hairs are long enough (at least 1/4 of an inch) to be grabbed by the wax.

The key to preventing ingrown hairs (an all too common downside of waxing for many) is to get the full hair follicle. The best way to do this is by keeping your skin taut and pulling the wax strip in the opposite direction of the way that your hair grows.

Sugaring, aka sugared wax or sugar gel, is generally safer because it’s applied to skin at room temperature (read: no risk of burning skin) and warms up with body heat. “Sugared ‘wax’ is not truly wax,” Dr. Murphy-Rose says. Rather, it’s a combination of sugar, lemon juice, and water. Some brands will infuse glycerin, fragrance, honey, or essential oils. Regardless of which brand you opt for, sugaring is water-soluble and easily rinses off the skin compared to hot wax, she says.

Sugaring and threading remove the hair follicle from the root but with less exfoliating, offering a gentler process, Dr. Okereke says. Though she warns that she's also had patients with coily-textured hair that still develop ingrown hairs and scarring from sugaring as well. The bottom line: "If you know you're prone to hyperpigmentation, ingrown hairs, or scarring, see a dermatologist to discuss the best hair removal practices for you," says Dr. Okereke.

#6767. Jensen Huang’s Shoes Were the Tell

I like men’s shoes and I’ve sold men’s shoes. A man’s footwear reveals facets of his public persona—and his private desires.

I spent much of Thursday staring at photographs of Jensen Huang’s shoes arranged in one soldierly line longer in both directions than the frame could hold. These dozen pairs—each different, all dazzling—were but a glimpse of his footwear armies. Jensen’s Hermès slip-ons and dozens of black leather jackets are not only his fashion signatures; they’re also proof of his decision to not waste precious energy deciding what clothes to wear each morning.

This is a philosophy I share with Jensen, and my version saves even more time and energy: Don’t change clothes. But I like men’s shoes, I’ve sold men’s shoes, and I believe that a man’s shoes reveal facets of his public persona and private desires.

In Jensen’s case, there’s no mystery. He’s a brilliant guy who was once a small, smart immigrant kid bullied and beaten at a Kentucky Baptist reform school that his uncle mistook for a boarding school. Now he’s 63, a five-foot-six AI titan standing in adorable—size 9 is my guess—multi-thousand-dollar slip-ons atop his $140 billion, with his dick out and dripping all over his kicks du jour.

And we are left to sort the tragedy, seek catharsis, and remind ourselves again—and again—that Jensen Huang’s downfall awaits any man who ignores the fundamental law of our own animal nature: Ven der putz shteht, der sechel licht in drert: When the penis stands, the brain goes to hell.

I make no moral judgment here; we have no basis or right to do so. The college sweetheart he married 41 years ago, along with their two grown children, are the only souls with standing in this case. But I think it’s only fair, in light of current reporting on Jensen’s three concurrent affairs—that’s two side pieces plus Princess Honey-Pot plus a wife—to reconsider that vast battalion of decision-saving slip-ons and what it says.

The shoes reveal a man with smallish feet whose penis was too busy making decisions to consider laces, love, or the welfare of his family and business. And there aren’t enough shoes, dollars, or women in the world to fill the hole in Jensen Huang.

The Latin word ‘invidia’ means envy. This, given Jensen’s shoes, is not coincidence or irony; it’s a weakling’s shamed confession. Hermes needs to cobble one last pair for Jensen Huang, long, bright red, and floppy.

#6764. The 9 Walking Shoes WH Editors Love the Most

These are the pairs we reach for again and again.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

It’s easy to underrate walking as one of the best things we can do for our fitness—after all, it doesn’t get your heart rate up like running or a HIIT workout. But that doesn’t mean you’re not getting tons of physical and mental health benefits from walking, whether you’re following a walking workout plan or not.

And perhaps one of the best things about walking is that it doesn’t require any specialized equipment or gear except a really good pair of comfy walking shoes. Luckily for you, we’re constantly testing walking shoes, and we’ve narrowed down all the options out there to a list of pairs we reach for again and again. We’ve used our testing experience, plus picked the brains of podiatrists and other experts to compile our favorites in this guide.

If you’ve logged even a couple of miles in a not-so-great pair of walking shoes, you know wearing the wrong footwear can make what started out as a nice walk turn into an uncomfortable slog fast (we’re talking blisters, achy arches, plantar fasciitis, and more).

The right walking shoes, on the other hand, can help you walk farther, feel happier while doing it, and gush to friends about your sneakers (ask us how we know). Plus, select styles can help prevent injury or overpronation (when your arches collapse and cause your foot to angle inward), says podiatrist Chanel Perkins, DPM, an assistant professor of foot and ankle podiatry in the Department of Orthopedic Surgery and Rehabilitation at the University of Texas Medical Branch.

After testing dozens of walking shoes for our 2026 Fitness Awards, the Adrenaline GTS 25 took the top spot for its near-perfect balance between cushioning and stability. Our staffers who tested it say it’s comfortable enough to walk in for hours. “It offers just the right amount of cushioning for a bouncy step without compromising on support for my flat, wide feet,” says WH beauty editor Nicole Saunders.

That comfort starts with the 25’s updated midsole, which feels plusher than previous models without losing an ounce of stability. It still features the brand’s signature GuardRails technology—firmer along the sides and softer through the center—to gently lock in your stride. In our testing, Brooks continues to set the standard for stability, making this a standout pick for anyone whose feet tend to roll inward while walking.

When it comes to fit, the 25 has a roomier toe box up front and a secure (but still comfortable) heel lock back, which helps cut down on friction and lowers your chances of blisters during long walks. The higher 10 mm drop creates a slightly forward-leaning stride that takes pressure off the calves and Achilles, making the 25 a smart pick for anyone with tight lower legs or Achilles sensitivity. If you prefer a flatter feel underfoot, though, we recommend something lower profile like the Hoka Mach 6.

At 9.5 ounces, it’s one of the heavier options on this list, but that added weight comes from the stability tech and structured testing. We barely noticed the difference, especially once we got moving.

Diana Rogers, DPM, a podiatric foot and ankle surgery specialist in Crystal Lake, Illinois, and Casey Pidich, MD, a podiatrist and foot and ankle surgeon based in the Bronx, New York, both recommend Hoka’s Clifton series to patients for arch support, so you know it's a good one.

Pidich loves that the design encourages great form—the sole ensures that your feet rock off the ground rather than push off your toes. Rogers specifically recommends the shoe for her plantar fasciitis (a condition where you feel pain in the arch) patients for this exact reason. “The lightweight feel on this shoe propels feet forward rather than making them do extra work,” she says.

WH style editor Shannon Zitz found in her testing that the foam absorbed impact well on both pavement and the treadmill. “My feet—unfortunately plagued by plantar fasciitis—never felt fatigued wearing this shoe,” she said, noting that her stride felt smooth and natural.

The Clifton 11 is available in wide and extra-wide sizing, making it a good fit for a wide variety of walkers.

Read More: Hoka Clifton 11 vs. Hoka Clifton Pro

For those who appreciate a good slip-on shoe, the Skechers Go Walk Flex Azriel’s lightweight, breathable mesh upper is great for sweaty feet, while hovering around a bargain price of about $60 (and on sale for cheaper sometimes). The ultra-cushioned midsole offers great arch support, too.

“This is a solid no-frills walking shoe that’s perfect for errands, quick hot girl strolls, or for walking your dog,” says WH style editor Shannen Zitz. “I love that it slips on easily while still having the appearance of a classic sneaker with bungee laces that never come undone.”

Zitz was skeptical about whether the slip-on would be supportive and secure enough, but the breathable, stretchy mesh offered surprisingly good compression around her feet.

“It keeps my feet locked in and has never caused any pain or blisters,” she says. “While I wouldn’t log miles in this shoe, it is cushioned and supportive enough for long periods of standing or casual wear.” However, she finds the style a bit orthopedic-looking, although the white colorway can look cute with jeans or dresses.

Asics is considered a classic, all-around great brand for walking, especially if you struggle with overpronation (ankles that roll inward) or have flat feet. Take the Gel-Kayano 32, which has thick foam borders on each side to encourage proper alignment and walking gait. Deep heel cups also help keep arches supported—there’s a reason this shoe made our best plantar fasciitis walking shoe guide.

Fitness contributor Nicolette Accardi is a huge fan of the firm, stiffer midsole that still maintains massive plushness with its signature gel cushioning system for superior shock absorption. “My landings feel like I’m hovering above ground, which says a lot given I stomp on hard pavement daily. I actually find it more cushioned than the Hoka Mach 6,” she says. The heel-to-toe drop is 8 millimeters, which Accardi says is a bit high for her liking, but ideal if you want to take all the pressure away from your arches.

The Hoka Mach 7 is Zitz’s personal favorite. While it’s technically a running shoe, she loves it for recovery and walking. “I’ve been living in this sneaker since returning from a week-long hiking trip where I logged about 20K steps per day,” she says. “It’s flexible, lightweight, and cushioned—it basically feels like a hug for my super tired feet.” The cushioning also doesn’t feel bulky compared to other Hoka models, such as the Bondi 8.

The previous iteration, the Mach 6, took home our 2025 Fitness Award for top walking shoe thanks to its blend of responsive cushioning, breathability, and lightweight construction—and the Mach 7 has built on that winning formula with an updated outer and grippier bottom. Zitz tested this shoe after it first dropped, walking more than 150 miles in it—and not once did she feel even a bit of foot fatigue or soreness.

Another brownie point? It comes in wide sizing. Some WH editors find standard Hoka sizing pretty narrow, but the wide fit has adequate space in the toe box, so you can walk for hours without experiencing any friction. For extra room overall, podiatrist Shital Sharma, DPM, a podiatrist at Pinewood Family Care Co. in New Jersey, recommends going up a half-size in all walking sneakers because your feet tend to swell and expand from physical activity.

Lined with mesh to make this an ultra-breathable sneaker, the Pegasus is a winner because it has a slim, low heel that Perkins says is awesome for maintaining a smooth walking cadence. We love that the 42 is lighter than the previous model and has a curvier toe box that gives more toe spring, a smoother ride, and a roomier fit.

WH Fitness Director Jacqueline Andriakos has worn countless versions of the Nike Pegasus for both running and walking and has logged hundreds of miles in it. “I find the sole a bit on the firmer side, and the mesh upper really hugs and forms to your individual foot shape,” she says. “The shoe feels perfectly snug and secure as you step without being too constricting.”

She does flag that the tongue sometimes gets folded up and tucked under the laces in a slightly uncomfortable way. “I fix this by being really diligent when I put the shoes on, making sure to pull the tongue taut and make sure it's lying flat before tying my shoes.”

The shoe is super breathable and lightweight, which makes it a great option for spring and summer, but less so for cold or wet weather. Overall, this is Andriakos’ go-to walking shoe recommendation for her friends and family—and so far she's only heard happy feedback from loved ones who have purchased a Pegasus shoe.

The latest edition of the Fresh Foam X 1080 wows us with its inclusive sizing, with narrow, standard, wide, and extra-wide fits available to accommodate all feet.

“I have a hard time finding walking shoes that are both cushioned and supportive enough for my high arches and sensitive balls of the feet, but that are also narrow enough,” Andriakos says. “This is absolutely perfect if you deal with any foot pain issues or struggle with sneakers being a bit too wide—I have walked over 100 miles in these at this point and have never even had a sore or tender spot from rubbing or chafing (they also required zero break-in period).”Andriakos also finds the laces are very secure and don’t come untied easily. The only downside? “When I’ve worn it on rainy days, I notice my feet will feel damp and it takes a bit to dry out.” To help combat that, she just throws it in the dryer (which, to be clear, isn’t recommended by the brand, but she found it hasn't damaged the silhouette!).

The On Cloudtilt Remix is built for walking, thanks to its sequence of collapsing “cloud” foam to deliver a smooth, plush ride. It has maximum cushioning and bounce, compared to lots of other walking shoes we’ve tested, making it a solid pick for walkers and casual runners who prefer a softer, cushier feel underfoot.

This style features Helion Superfoam (a blend of firm and flexible foam components) that delivers bounce without a lot of bulk. The New Balance 608 V5 Trainer has over 38,000 reviews on Amazon, and for good reason. The brand integrated its signature ABZORB midsole (a blend of substantial foam cushioning with compression resistance), which is designed to lessen the blow on joints when walking.

The 608 has tons of shock absorption which makes it great for neutral to high arches. But since it lacks stability features, it’s not our top choice for flat feet. We do like that it comes in narrow, standard, and wide sizing.

Ideally, walking shoes will assist with your gait (your walking pattern) instead of just cushioning impact. Your shoe should also be somewhat stiff—the more it bends, the less stability it will have.

To test the flexibility, hold the shoe at the toe box and heel counter and try to fold the shoe in half by pushing on its ends to see how sturdy it is. If it folds in half or twists with ease, it’s too flexible, says podiatrist Elizabeth Daughtry, MD, a podiatrist and foot and ankle specialist with Foot and Ankle Specialists of the Mid-Atlantic in Cary, North Carolina.

She also adds that seeking out a shoe with a boat-like (rockered) shape, rigid midsole, and firm heel counter can help stabilize feet, control overpronation (collapsing arches), and prevent heel striking.

Look for walking shoes that have an outsole with more rigidity in the midsole for stability. Different feet have different needs, so it’s important to have insoles that can be removed in case you use orthotics. “Most shoes don’t offer adequate support alone,” says Dr. Daughtry. “You should supplement most shoes with a more custom-molded orthotic, which your local podiatrist can make for you, as this provides adequate support and helps with shock absorption, while also allowing for additional cushioning.”

Testing footwear is something we’re a little obsessive about, and we’re testing walking shoes on a constant basis. Over the past year alone, we’ve tested over 100 walking shoes, paying attention to fit, comfort, support, and durability. To narrow it down even further, we also consulted podiatrists and certified trainers about their favorite picks.

Additional reporting by Nicolette Accardi.

#6753. 30 High-Protein Meals That Are Just as Easy to Whip Up as They Are Delicious

So much more exciting than grilled chicken breast.

As a society, we’re in our protein era, and that’s a good thing. The macronutrient is super important for building and repairing muscles, everyday body functionality, and immune and hormone health. Plus, it helps keep you full.

Since it's so important, it's also no surprise that you need quite a bit of it, even though pulling that off might get a little tricky sometimes. While the recommended dietary allowance for protein has remained the same for 30 years—at 0.36 grams per pound of total body weight, or 0.8 grams per kilogram—research now suggests that we need more like one gram per pound. (Psst: that higher number is especially important for anyone who regularly hits the gym.) One easy way to make sure you get there? Following high-protein recipes, where all the calculations are already done for you.

The Women's Health kitchen has you covered. Every single one of the high-protein recipes below packs in at least 20 grams of the macro. These high-protein meals offer a variety of beyond-delicious ways to get more of it into your diet. From high-protein breakfast recipes to steak dinners and meatless options, there's something for everyone. Ready to get cooking?

Join WH+ for access to our High-Protein Meal Prep Manual, the WH Protein Playbook, and the Ultimate Protein + Fiber Nutrition Guide.

#6749. Wonderskin vs. Sacheu: Which Viral Lip Stain Is Better?

After testing both for a full month, I have thoughts.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Hot take: Lip stains are the most important lippies to have in your makeup routine—especially if you hate reapplying and want the color to stay all day. There is no shortage of lip stain options, but two in particular have been duking it out on TikTok. Both the Sacheu Lip Liner Stay-n and the Wonderskin Lip Stain have gone super viral. If you’ve been wondering if one is better than the other, you’re not alone. This is why, in the name of beauty journalism, I decided to test them both to give you all the deets and my honest reviews.

For those of you who are unfamiliar, these two lip stains stay put for literal HOURS. They're not as opaque as lipstick—the color is more sheer than saturated. Plus, there are multiple ways to use each formula: They can be worn as long-lasting lip liners (because, let’s be real, I have yet to find a pencil liner that truly lasts all day) or as an all-over tint under a shimmery lip gloss or lip balm.

The real question is, which one deserves a spot in your makeup bag? Keep reading to find out. The answer may surprise you!

Wonderskin Wonder Blading All-Day Lip Stain

Confession: I rolled my eyes when I first saw this deep purple formula on TikTok, but once I tried it, I was hooked. This lip stain applies just like a typical lip product—except for its unique color. Don’t worry, a stunning flush is revealed after letting it sit for one minute. Once set, you simply wipe it off with a damp cloth or tissue to reveal a gorgeous lip color.

This pairs seamlessly with any lip oil, gloss, or balm, so you can layer on your favorite hydrating formula without worrying about the color fading away. The only downside? You need a wet tissue or cloth to remove the purple layer, so be sure to apply this at the start of your makeup routine to avoid accidentally wiping away other products.

I really love this stain when I know I have an event or long meal ahead of me (filled with multiple drinks, greasy food, lip gloss applications, and maybe some smooches). Somehow, it’s always still perfectly intact by the end of my night. And of course, we have some more thoughts from Cosmo’s beauty editor, Beth Gillette:

"Wonderskin is definitely the better lip stain if you want to cover your entire lip. It's also available in a gazillion shades—32 to be exact!—which is def helpful if you want a wide range, like a red lip that won't budge. I definitely prefer Sacheu’s peel-off format; however, wiping this lip stain off definitely helps me avoid any mistakes I might make when applying."—BG

Do you know how much your favorite singer sweats on stage? It’s a miracle any makeup stays put under those intense lights. I knew this lip stain was the real deal with Billie Eilish shared that she uses it. In her now mega-viral GRWM video, she over lines her lips for a plump, defined pout that still looks pristine by the end of her concert.

The formula of this lip stain goes on as a deeper version of your chosen shade. I recommend applying it at the start of your routine because it needs to dry down for about 10 to 15 minutes for the magic to work. Once set, you simply peel it off in one satisfying go to reveal a wash of long-lasting color.

I applied the stain every morning for weeks and at the end of every day—after three meals and countless snacks—the color looked just as good as it did 12 hours prior. There was zero smudging, smearing, or feathering either. Need more convincing? Check out what contributing beauty editor Mary Honkus has to say:

“Not gonna lie, I was skeptical about every aspect of this lip stain. The peel-off application was totally foreign, and I’ve never ever experienced a lip product that lasts all day. It took a few tries to really understand the magic behind this. I grew to love the fun (and satisfying) peeling step that reveals the prettiest stain. It truly lasts all day, too—through workouts and multiple meals—without fading or feathering. I will say this works much better as a liner than an all-over lip color. I don’t get an even peel when I applied it all over my lips. As a lip liner, though, I can’t live without it.”—MH

Both the Sacheu and Wonderskin lip stains are truly amazing products, fully worthy of their viral hype. The Sacheu applicator offers more precision and is an excellent value at just $14, while Wonderskin wins when it comes to shade range. The only setback to both? Removal. Sacheu’s stain needs a full 15 minutes before you can peel it off, while Wonderskin's requires a wet tissue to wipe away—so you have to pick your struggle! That said, both deliver impressive, all-day wear, so the best choice comes down to your personal preference and budget.

Mary Honkus is a contributor for Cosmopolitan with over eight years of experience researching, writing, and editing beauty stories. She’s an authority in all beauty categories and hoards lip products. For this story, she tested both the Sacheu and Wonderskin lip stains to bring you her honest expert opinion.

#6745. Michelle Yeoh on Strength, Stunts, and What Makes Us Human

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

At 64, the star of Blade Runner 2099 is more intentional about how she moves, trains, and takes risks—and she says her mindset is better, too.

An action hero used to have to be big and brawny and brusque. Michelle Yeoh is none of those things—but you feel her presence and power the instant you meet her. She is kind, warm, and lovely, but she’s also intense…in a really good way. This is a person who listens closely yet also seems ready to launch into a wheelhouse kick at any moment.

Starring as Olwen, a bioengineered artificial human (or “replicant,” as they’re called in the Blade Runner universe, which includes the new series Blade Runner 2099, out November 25 on Amazon Prime Video), the 64-year-old global megastar literally wrestles with people and replicants alike on-screen.

But in real life, she dances with even larger issues. Like what separates humans from the bot world, how we define strength beyond the physical, why we all need to stretch every morning, and, finally, how she gets over a very specific fear. (If you’ve ever watched her in movies like Tomorrow Never Dies or even Everything Everywhere All At Once, you’ll understand how shocking this particular fear is for her. The solution is one six-letter word…and you’ll just have to read on to find out what that is.)

I sat down with Michelle—who is classically trained in ballet and does many of her own martial arts stunts—in NYC for a wide-ranging convo that starts with robots and ends with what she calls the “Michelle prayer.”

Women’s Health: What was it like to play a replicant, and how did you think about preparing and training for that mindset?

Michelle Yeoh: We have a brilliant writer, Silka Luisa, who had thought out the story for Blade Runner 2099 [a continuation of the original series] and where it has gone. All these replicants are now in charge of a Los Angeles that is no longer what we know. But it’s very interesting because [the question that arises is] What is humanity? The replicants do give the humans hope. As long as you’re good, as long as you do things the way we want them to be handled, you could go to a better place, because now Los Angeles is not exactly the most habitable place.

Replicants are made to look human. In fact, they are flesh and blood, except they’re stronger, faster. They are able to withstand the new worlds. But empathy is what gives you away as a human. Replicants are born without that emotion. They’re born with memories. They’re given memories to keep them sort of happy and at peace with what they are. Are you born with humanity or does it come with experience and as you live your life with compassion and kindness?

WH: You said that you’re excited for people to see the series and to share ideas and to talk about it. Is that why? The humanity complexity?

Michelle: The worst is they say, “Oh, yeah, it was interesting.…” What is “just” interesting? Hopefully you go home, you think about it, and then you come back and the next day at work or in school, you’re chatting with your friends— “Well, what are you? A replicant or a human? What would you rather be?”

WH: How did you prepare, physically, for this role?

Michelle: I think the older you get, the more you have to do to keep up your muscle strength, to keep your muscles there. Without that, you start walking differently. I have the advantage of understanding my body a lot more because I’ve been doing this for the last, well, ever since I was born. I was dancing, doing sports, everything. I do core-strengthening exercises and tai chi every day.

The core was the most important part of the training because we wanted to nail the way Olwen moved. It was with precision. Because if you want to move with motivation, it has to have that strength, that inner strength, rather than just speed. So when you move, it’s almost like you glide. As a replicant, all the moves are sharp, precise, because they’re so strong and powerful. Actually, when you take a human down, it’s too easy. I think all exercises are core-focused. You can’t lift weights or do pilates without working on the core first. Everything has to start from there.

WH: Has your own relationship to risk changed over the years? Are there stunts that you used to do in your 30s that now you’re like “nah,” or do you actually feel more powerful and in control?

Michelle: I think that’s really interesting and a good way of putting it. Feeling powerful doesn’t mean being reckless and silly. I feel more powerful in the sense that I have a better assessment of the risk factors. But naturally, because when I was in my 20s, when I first started out, you’re brave and foolish and you’re also so fit. And I was really, really, really fit at that time because I didn’t have any crazy injuries. And, so, fearless is a good word, but fearless and stupid is not good. That’s a bad combination.

Sometimes the smallest stunts are the worst because you think they’re easy, and then you don’t pay full attention. You’re not focused enough. You’re focused, but you’re like, “Yeah, yeah, yeah. I’ll get it done.” And that’s when something bad will happen.

In the past, when you are beginning, you listen and you do what they tell you to do, and then you have a lot to prove because you don’t want them to think that you’re unable or you’re not capable, so you do it. But now you go, “How about we…” And then you can speak with power because you know your character. And then I’ll go, “But Olwen won’t do it like that.” And who is going to challenge me?

WH: I understand that you became a bit of a mentor to Hunter Schafer. So talk to us a little about the stunt piece.… Did you help her figure out how to have that voice for herself?

Michelle: To be honest, when I look at her, I go, “Oh my god, I see my young self.” When they show her something, she’ll just throw herself into it because she wants to do it. She’s eager to do it. But then I have to keep reminding her, “You don’t have the same kind of training the stunt people have. So when you can’t do it, it doesn’t mean that you’re not good.”

What I do is try to remind her, like, “Hunter, knee pads, Hunter, elbow pads. What is the stunt that you’re doing? Get them on.” Because sometimes you go, like, No, no, no, it’s okay. It’s not okay. Get it on.

It’s a joy to work with her. It’s just a joy to be with her, which sometimes can be difficult because we’re supposed to be against each other in Blade Runner 2099. I mean, she’s a human pretending to be a replicant, pretending to be a blade runner. But somehow their missions intertwine, so they have no choice but to learn to work together. So we have a lot of fun.

WH: What are your nonnegotiables? What are the things you, Michelle, must do every single day for your health that you are consistent with?

Michelle: Water, sunblock, and a lot of stretching. That is a must.

I do tai chi, I do my ballet and tai chi. I’ve been doing that for a long time now. I try to do that every day because I’ve unfortunately had injuries in the past—my back, my knees, my shoulder. I don’t know where I don’t have an injury. As I’m in bed, I do a lot of stretching. When I wake up, the first thing I do in my bed is to go through the motions so that the body wakes up with the mind and everything comes into place.

I think that’s what you have to find: your own moves [or routine] so that you don’t give yourself an excuse when you go, “Oh, it’s raining. No time to go to the gym.” So you just do it even in the bed, you’re stretching, you’re doing all those core exercises to wake up the mind, to wake up the abs, the back, and the legs, everything. And that helps when we’re actually on-set because then I can step into it and know I am strong and ready to go.

I love being outside, going on hikes. Then if I can’t do that, I’m on the elliptical, because I think cardio is still very important. But now, with time, I do a lot more weights. I’m picking up dumbbells or water bottles, whatever works. You have to combine the strengthening of muscles with cardio exercise.

WH: I’m curious if there’s something that is better now in your 60s than it was in your 20s—anything?

Michelle: I think the only thing that’s a little bit better might be this up here [motions to head].

I do a fairly decent job at keeping up the energy, keeping up the spirits, because I also think it’s the inner strength that is very important. Your mind is very important. If you don’t have the inner peace, it’s very hard to keep control of your body because negative vibes are the worst thing for any human being. When you have negative vibes around you, in you, then you’re down. I honestly believe that because you’ve gone through so many experiences, you’re better for it. I wouldn’t give that up.

Michelle: I have a fear of heights. I know. See, I’m claustrophobic and have a fear of heights.

Michelle: But the magic word is action. Camera rolling…action. And I am focused. Fearless. It’s really, really bizarre. If I don’t hear the word action, then it’s a no go. If I’m in the mall, you know, sometimes you have those walkways that look over the first floor. I walk right in the middle of those. I’m taking no risk. But once action is said on set, I’m good.

WH: You mentioned a mantra. What, exactly, is it?

Michelle: When I wake up, even before opening my eyes and stretching, I sort of have my own little mantra to wake myself up. I try to talk to my body, thank my body, apologize for anything I’ve done bad, and then welcome everything that’s good because I’m deserving of it.

I don’t sit there and meditate. I don’t understand that at all. Just as long as you feel it yourself that I have what I need for me…because everybody is different.

And that’s why I start my day with a prayer, a mantra. I guess you could call it my own Michelle prayer. Be thankful and be willing to accept anything good that comes your way. I think we have to look at ourselves and have self-worth because I think the inner strength, the inner peace, that sense of compassion and kindness and contentment, is very important. Because if you’re always wanting, needing, and thinking about something else, someone else, then you’re always in conflict, first of all, with yourself.

WH: What does the word strength mean to you? When you hear that word, what’s the definition that comes to mind?

Michelle: I think when you hear that word, first of all, it’s always very top layer. You walk into the gym, I have strength, I have power. I put on a power suit. That’s powerful. But for me, true strength is right in there [points to heart]. Showing weakness is strength. Doing a lot of things that people don’t understand, but for you, when you make a choice, it’s strength. There are so many ways of looking at it, and I don’t think there’s one definition unless you check the encyclopedia and they tell you what it is.

WH: And then we’ll just ignore that and go with the Michelle Yeoh definition anyway.

*This interview has been edited and condensed for clarity*
