#6700. The Fall Essentials You Need According to 4 of the Most Stylish Guys We Know

Grails, staples, and everyday beaters abound.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Fall has the singular ability to turn even the most Spartan man into a bit of a style savant. It’s like a wolf awakens the second that temperatures start to drop. Suddenly you see an Instagram post from the junior analyst below you; he must fancy himself a budding A$AP Rocky, because he’s rocking a statement scarf. Your brother-in-law is wearing a turtleneck. Your buddy is in white jeans. Every day, you zone out in dumb stakeholder meetings and fantasize about a new jacket or that pair of buttery-soft leather loafers you saw on a dude at the grocery store.

It’s because fall invites more—more layers, more textures. It can be freeing. No more worrying if that T-shirt will become a body scan of where and how you sweat; no more worrying if those pants will suffocate you. But ... it can be too much. How should you layer? How can you get funky for the holiday party without looking like an asshole? What brands are even worth buying?

We answer all those questions elsewhere in this magazine, but today we want you to take a breath. Keep it simple. How? By following some good examples.

A lot of the best-dressed guys right now are trimming down their wardrobes in favor of focusing on the basics. “This fall I’ve tried to trim the fat and make do with a much smaller wardrobe,” says actor, content creator, and friend of the magazine Albert Muzquiz—better known online by his handle @edgyalbert. “I only have a few shirts, a few pants, and a couple jackets—and I feel like I’m dressing better than ever.”

So instead of lobbing a bunch of meaningless trends, or -cores, at you, we decided to send you to some of the best-dressed men of the moment. You’ve heard enough from us; here’s what they’re feeling this fall.

Jake Mueser, founder of the eponymous bespoke suiting brand J. Mueser, has a handful of fall essentials. That cohort includes a cream turtleneck, which “you can throw on with anything,” as well as a classic tweed or herringbone blazer—same idea. They’re easy but plenty damn sexy.

Mueser also extols the virtues of a classic flannel suit—perhaps like the one he makes. “It works with everything from a denim shirt to a tie,” he says. “I like to break it up, wearing the jacket with vintage light-wash Wrangler cowboy cuts.” (A move we wholeheartedly endorse.)

J. Mueser Waverly Herringbone Virgin Wool-Tweed Blazer

J. Mueser Ivory Chunky Merino Roll-Neck Sweater

J. Mueser Charcoal Churchill Stripe Flannel Waverly Suit

There are a few reasons Drew Thomas can’t stop recommending merino sweaters—the thin, almost jersey sort—to his more than 500,000 TikTok followers. “You can get so much wear out of one,” he says. “You can layer it in the fall, wear it on its own on a slightly warmer day, or tie it around your shoulders. It works in the spring too, and even on a summer night when it gets a little cooler.”

Another seasonally versatile piece Thomas will stand ten toes down for is cream pants. In fact, Thomas likes to wear his all through fall. He keeps a few pairs on constant rotation. “I feel like the cream breaks up those darker fall colors and lets them stand out more,” he says.

When it comes to his next fall investment, Thomas is taking cues from the British sartorial invasion (“It never really went away, but it definitely feels like it’s having a moment again”) and looking to Barbour. “Something timeless and practical that I can wear for years and that only gets better with age,” he says. “I love the idea of owning something that develops character over time and could eventually be passed down to someone else. Those are the kinds of pieces I feel are worth investing in.”

J. Crew Relaxed-Fit Double-Pleated Corduroy Pant

“A down vest is my favorite fall garment,” Esquire contributor Christopher Fenimore told us. “It’s an easy outerwear piece to layer stuff under. I run hot, and I like having parts of my body exposed to the elements.”

We second that, but more predictably he highlights the entire category of shirting. Jackets and layers get a lot of shine, but this is the layer that, no matter how much temperatures fluctuate, will be on the skin. It’s a great time to get a few good oxford-cloth button-downs, polos, or beefier tees.

This time of year is also a great time to get clinical. “I assess things I have that might need updates, in terms of fit and silhouette or different fabrications depending on how I’d wear them,” he says. That can be rebuying your preferred undershirt or socks, or it could be a good time to jump on that thinner, more versatile merino sweater wave.

“Fall is the best time to break in a new pair of denim,” Muzquiz says. Wear it hard enough now and “it’ll be soft enough and possibly breathable enough for wear during the spring and parts of the summer.” No better time to get something esoteric.

When it comes to fall shoes, Muzquiz is into a classic six-inch lace-up boot. “There are so many bullshit brands out there that are trying to fool you with budget options,” he says. “Just get a pair of Red Wing Heritage if you’re starting out or Viberg if you really wanna flex.”

Muzquiz also finds himself taking part in the “buttery, delicate loafer movement” that’s been picking up steam as of late. The Yardee Moccasin loafers from Vinny’s are his pick of the bunch.

Viberg Navvy Boot - Brandy Hatch Grain Calf

#6697. Luke Grimes Didn’t Know If Marshals Would Work Without Kayce’s Wife

“He wants his little slice of heaven,” Grimes told Esquire when Yellowstone ended. “It’s that simple.”

Coming back home isn’t easy, especially for Luke Grimes. The Yellowstone actor spent all of the hit Taylor Sheridan drama as Kayce Dutton, a man trying to leave the past behind and move on from the sins of his father, family patriarch John Dutton (Kevin Costner). But when the western ended and Grimes had to say goodbye to the cast, crew, and his onscreen wife Kelsey Asbille (who played Monica Dutton), he really thought it was for the last time.

“Hopefully everyone can see that it was time,” Grimes told Esquire when Yellowstone ended. “There’s always talks of spin-offs. I’d do it [but] I just don’t understand how it would work once the story ends. Kayce wants to cowboy and be happy with his family. He doesn’t want to kill people anymore. He doesn’t want the weight of a huge mega ranch that isn’t sustainable in today’s times. He wants his little slice of heaven. It’s that simple.”

Paramount offered Grimes the option to return anyway on Marshals, a spin-off of Yellowstone that sees Kayce Dutton join the U.S. Marshals service in Montana. Reportedly, they won him over after pitching that they would take everything from Kayce so that he would be forced to start again. First on the chopping block? His wife, Monica.

“The thing I was most worried about doing Marshals was doing it without Kelsey,” Grimes told ScreenRant during an interview for season 2 last week. “I just couldn’t wrap my head around Kayce without Monica. That was the story. The rest of Yellowstone was about people fighting over land, and I just loved my wife. I had my own thing going on, and so I didn’t know what that would even look like.”

Eventually he came around to the idea. “Once I sort of, you know, could wrap my head around the fact that that was going to open up all these new storylines,” he admitted, “we just [had] to make sure we do honor [their life together] and don’t try to skip over it or pretend it didn’t happen.”

Marshals season 2 continues to air this fall, as Kayce Dutton explores a potential new relationship with fellow Marshal Andrea Cruz (Ash Santos). Sometime this season, former Yellowstone fan favorite Jimmy (Jefferson White) will also make an appearance.

#6693. Behold: The Starbucks Red Cup the Year You Were Born

Or, you know, the year you started drinking coffee...

Obviously nothing beats a frothy PSL on a crisp autumn day (bonus points if you are unabashedly wearing UGGs), but we're here to talk about an even more iconic cozy szn tradition: the annual Starbucks red cup (or the Starbucks holiday cup). As anyone who grew up in the early 2000s knows, Starbucks red cups were an absolute hallmark of the holidays. Truly, there was nothing like begging your mom to drop you off at the mall so you could lurk around, brand-new red cup in hand, and hit up, IDK, American Eagle Outfitters.

Red cups have been going strong since the late ’90s—they launched in 1997, to be exact, when they weren't even red, but more on that later. And every year, the cup has a brand-new design…sometimes multiple! So, now that the new 2026 red cups have been unveiled (scroll all the way down to see them!), the time has come to find out which Starbucks red cup sparked joy the year you were born.

Before we begin, apologies to all the millennials out there, but red cups simply weren't a thing from 1981 to 1996. However, please feel free to personally identify with the holiday cup of your choice (or the cup from the year you started drinking coffee)—OR just take a cozy walk down memory lane. Godspeed and have a jolly good day.

#6692. 11 Winter Colognes You Can Get for Under $100

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Smelling great doesn’t have to cost a fortune.

There’s never been a better time to get into personal fragrance. The cologne market is booming with frequent new launches from luxury fashion houses and perfumeries, yes—but it’s also filled with tons of great scents from more accessible and affordable brands. And with the temperatures finally dropping below 50°F in New York and Thanksgiving coming up next week, we’re officially in winter mode. That means getting our puffer coats out of storage and changing up our seasonal fragrance rotation.

So out with the fresh, aquatic, citrusy fragrances that belong on the beach and in with the warm, spicy, smoky scents that make the wintertime feel cozy. We’re talking cardamom and cloves, smoky tobacco and incense, sweet vanilla and smooth amber. Want to capture the scent of sitting by the fireplace and sipping a hot cocoa while wearing a cashmere sweater? These scents will do that—and at a fraction of the cost of a designer fragrance. We rounded up our picks for the winter that all come in at under $100, so you can save the big bucks for holiday shopping.

If you’ve checked out any of our other affordable-cologne roundups, you won’t be surprised to see Cremo at the top of our list here. We talk about the brand a lot, but it’s for a good reason: Cremo’s colognes are some of our favorites out there, and they literally cost under $25 each. Spice & Black Vanilla is our favorite spicy scent from the lineup, and the warm blend of cardamom, vanilla, tobacco, and woods is perfect for winter. Wear it for a cold-weather date night, a fancy dinner, or really any occasion.

Want a decent cologne mist you can easily buy on Amazon for just $13? Old Spice has been around for almost 90 years—yes, it may remind you of your dad, but it’s been around for so long because it’s a classic that just works. But shaking things up a bit, the brand released a new collection of cologne mists earlier this year, which includes this Cashmere & Vanilla Wood scent bursting with spices and powdery iris. For $13, you might as well order a few.

One of our favorite boozy colognes (and all-time favorite colognes in general) is Jazz Club from Maison Margiela’s Replica line. It’s one you’ve probably heard of and read about many times in our fragrance roundups, and since the 1 oz bottle is only $89, I just couldn’t leave it off this list. With rum, tobacco, and vanilla bean at the center, Jazz Club is the olfactory equivalent of sipping a cocktail in a smoke-filled bar. In other words, it’s the perfect sexy winter scent.

Gap recently introduced a new addition to its revitalized fragrance line (originally from the ’90s): Bliss. The gourmand EDP adds some creamy sweetness to the otherwise mostly fresh and earthy collection, and it came just in time for winter. It delights the senses with notes of butterscotch, cocoa bean, coconut milk, and vanilla bourbon, and it’s practically making my mouth water just thinking about it.

Huron has only five different scents, but it’s a true example of a brand that puts quality over quantity. I love each and every one of the brand’s EDPs, and while the marine Sea Salt + Driftwood and Original Citrus + Eucalyptus are both fresh and perfect for the summer, the Sandalwood + Black Pepper fragrance is definitely our winter choice. It’s warm, woody, and spicy, with some airy florals at the heart and rich amber and more woods at the base.

If you like the scent of Tom Ford’s Oud Wood but can’t get past that $300 price tag, we hear you. That’s why House of Twist made Wood No. 28; it’s not exactly Oud Wood, but it’s close enough. And for just $48, we’ll take it. The fragrance is a nice blend of nutty cardamom, oud wood, vanilla, and creamy sandalwood—a combination pretty much anyone can get behind.

Making the cut at $99 is Phlur’s newest fragrance: Heavy Cream. It’s a rich gourmand that’s balanced out by some bright citrus and airy florals at the top and heart of the fragrance, so it doesn’t get too overwhelmingly heavy. It definitely has the notes you’d expect of a fragrance inspired by heavy cream, like vanilla, amber, and creamy musks.

Fine’ry is another one of our go-to brands when it comes to affordable colognes we actually like. And this winter, our top choice from the lineup is Smoke & Leather, which smells like—you guessed it—smoky tobacco and rich leather with some woody undertones. It definitely brings the heat on a cold day.

Boy Smells is known for pairing traditionally masculine and feminine scent notes for truly unisex fragrances that anyone can enjoy. Hinoki Fantôme is a fan favorite thanks to its blend of spices, woods, and smoke, which are underscored by earthy oakmoss, orris, and patchouli. You really can’t put this fragrance in one category, because it spans so many. It’s a complex fragrance that really works, especially for the winter.

Earthy scents are chameleons that can work in any season, so it really just matters what they’re paired with. In the instance of Stetson’s Free Rein from its new Untamed collection, the pairing of earthy notes of wild verbena, sweetgrass, prairie moss, and patchouli with vetiver smoke and red amber makes it a warm, rich fragrance that adds some heat to your cold-weather outfit.

Florals are, generally speaking, made for spring and summer—but sometimes you get a gorgeous blend of florals with woods, musks, or amber, and it becomes perfect for wintertime. That’s exactly what Lafco has done with its Golden Morning scent: It’s got light floral notes of apple blossom, lavender, violet leaf, and rose, which all play nicely with a rich, sweet base of woods, honey, amber, and musks.

#6689. The 8 Best Salicylic Acid Cleansers for Acne, According to Dermatologists

These sudsy picks are the first step to clearer skin.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Oh, hi, hello! I'm assuming you've stumbled upon this page because (a) you'd do anything to cure your acne, and (b) there's an equally good chance you've f*cked with your face (see: red, inflamed skin) while trying to do so in the past. Well, allow me to introduce you to salicylic acid—the powerhouse chemical exfoliant that effectively clears out blackheads, whiteheads, and excess oil. The best and easiest way to start incorporating the ingredient into your skincare routine is with a salicylic acid cleanser.

Listen, as easy as it is to go to the drugstore and pick up a new salicylic acid cleanser, I encourage you to take a beat. While the ingredient is gentler than other acne remedies—like benzoyl peroxide—salicylic acid can still cause dryness and irritation for those with sensitive skin. It also comes in many different concentrations and formulas, so finding the right product for you and your skin type is important. That’s where I come in. I spent the last year testing the top salicylic acid cleansers to see how they reacted on my own skin, and consulted the pros on all the need-to-knows. (Hi, board-certified dermatologists Annie Chiu, MD, Lucy Chen, MD, Cheryl Burgess, MD, and Loren Krueger, MD!)

Keep reading for the full breakdown of the best salicylic acid cleansers—according to team Cosmo’s testing—along with tips from dermatologists on choosing the best salicylic acid cleansers, how to incorporate one into your skincare routine, and what to look for before you buy.

This doesn’t just cut through makeup and grime for a satisfying cleanse, it also helps reduce oil, acne, and redness to get you one step closer to a smooth, clear, radiant complexion. We especially appreciate that it addresses multiple types of breakouts, including stubborn cystic and hormonal acne, with 1 percent salicylic acid, making it gentle yet extremely effective.

One Cosmo editor says: “On the rare days I wash my face in the morning, I like a no-frills cleanser that’ll degrease whatever oils spewed onto my face overnight. This one, with 1 percent salicylic acid and anti-inflammatory zinc, does this flawlessly without making my skin feel tight.”—BETH GILLETTE, BEAUTY EDITOR

It utilizes salicylic acid naturally found in willow bark extract, along with caprylol salicylic acid, an LHA, to gently refine texture. We noticed enhanced glow from the first use, and quickly added this to our permanent acne-fighting lineup.

One Cosmo editor says: “I’m familiar with BHA (aka salicylic acid), but LHA is new for me, and based on this cleanser, I think I love it. It chemically sloughs away dead skin to reduce clogged pores and bumpy texture, but it’s not too strong or intense for sensitive skin.”—BG

It still manages to clear out clogged pores without much effort, helping them look smaller in the long run. In addition to salicylic acid, which helps clear clogged pores, this formula features a zinc compound to tackle oil, as well as allantoin to soothe my skin—both key for anyone dealing with acne."—IMAN BALAGAM, CONTRIBUTING BEAUTY WRITER

Salicylic acid is a beta hydroxy acid (BHA) that comes from bark willow, a natural substance, making it a natural ingredient, explains Dr. Burgess. It's most commonly known for "helping clear pores of dirt, reducing acne-causing sebum production at the follicular level, and dissolving dead skin cells," says Dr. Chiu. Because it has anti-inflammatory properties, penetrates deep into the pores, and acts as a chemical exfoliant, it can greatly help reduce mild acne while working to prevent future breakouts, Dr. Chiu adds.

In dermatology or skincare, the ingredient salicylic acid is most commonly used in a topical form. It is approved anywhere up to 2 percent by the FDA for over-the-counter sales and the mass market, making it readily available for purchase in many different forms. If you need a higher dosage for specific concerns, it can be prescribed by a doctor or dermatologist under their supervision and care.

Salicylic acid can also be used to treat other skin issues and concerns due to its exfoliating properties. They include, but are not limited to:

According to Dr. Chiu, the cleanser you choose should depend on your skin type, age, and primary skin concern. A salicylic acid cleanser comes in two forms: 1) a soapy and lathery consistency, which is best for oily skin, and 2) a soapless preparation, which is best for drier skin, explains Dr. Burgess. Then, depending on your skin type, she adds, you can choose between 0.5, 1, or a 2 percent salicylic acid concentration. It is important to note that the higher the concentration of salicylic acid in the cleanser, the more drying it can be.

Although a lot of us may be used to throwing on, lathering up, and immediately rinsing a cleanser off, Dr. Burgess has a recommendation to get the full effect of the salicylic acid cleanser: You want to suds it up, put it on your skin, and let it sit on there for maybe a minute or two. By doing this extra step, the top layer of the skin is softened, and the active ingredient can penetrate just a little bit more. You'll subsequently see more response versus just washing your face and then abruptly rinsing, she says.

When using a salicylic acid cleanser, it is important to let your skin be the guide, explains Dr. Krueger. "I recommend starting the use of a salicylic acid cleanser either every other morning or each morning," she says, adding that using the cleanser too frequently at the beginning of your treatment can potentially lead to irritation. "If you are tolerating well at once daily yet have room for improvement in your skin, you can bounce it up to twice daily," she says.

"Everyone's skin can react differently," reminds Dr. Chen "Common side effects include burning, peeling, dry, flaky skin, and inflammation, especially at the start of treatment," she says, adding that if any of these effects worsen or continue, make sure to consult your doctor because sometimes, all you may need to change is the amount or how frequently you're using it. If you are experiencing dryness, apply a gentle, fragrance-free face moisturizer after cleansing.

She’s an authority in all beauty categories and prides herself on her skin. For this story, she interviewed dermatologists and polled team Cosmo to discover their tried-and-true salicylic acid cleansers.

She feels especially knowledgeable about salicylic acid cleansers because she regularly tests and analyzes formulas on her own skin for efficacy. #6684. The Masculinity Defenders Are Already Whining About Robert Pattinson’s James Bond

Every incel with a YouTube account thinks R-Patz isn’t manly enough to be the face of a film franchise that contains a movie called Octopussy.

After an exhaustive and exhausting years-long casting process, Robert Pattinson has been announced as the new James Bond, and right on cue, the masculinity defenders are being super normal about it.

I thought the big takeaway was that we have the first James Bond with a cute little social media nickname (which led me to wonder whether we’d have been calling Pierce Brosnan “P-Broz” if there had been a Twitter in 1995). But nope: the deal is that R-Patz is not manly enough to be the face of a film franchise that contains a movie called Octopussy.

On a YouTube show called Dan Wootton: Outspoken, special guest and self-described “right-wing comedian” Leo Kearse said Pattinson is “Not a man! He doesn’t have a hairy chest, no drink problem, he’s never bought a fridge.” Okay! Point by point: 1) Daniel Craig was smooth as a baby seal. 2) Watch The Lighthouse if you think R-Patz can’t convincingly put them away. 3) What?

Kearse, ignorant and undeterred as a real man ought to be, continues: “He probably still sleeps in a car shaped like a Ferrari, with Powerpuff Girls sheets.” Also he and Wootton both seem to be calling him “Robert Patterson” throughout. Great stuff. I mean this, Leo Kearse: you are the funniest right-wing comedian I’ve ever seen.

Interesting, except that the next Bond movie will be written by British screenwriter Steven Knight, who wrote the ultra-violent Eastern Promises, created the epic gangster series Peaky Blinders, and co-created Who Wants to Be a Millionaire?, which doesn’t have anything to do with James Bond or the broader masculinity conversation per se, but goddamn what a career.

In The Atlantic, Tom Nichols complained that “Pattinson lacks the manly gravitas …Pattinson is sensitive; he broods; he’s conflicted.” He concludes, “I imagine that when the mad criminal offers him a mint julep, Pattinson will scowl and explain that alcohol and sugar are bad for you.” Listen: if there’s anything more masculine than imagining a scenario and then getting angry at it, it’s fretting endlessly about what is and is not masculine.

Right under a piece whining that “the left-wing bias of The Grammys is on full display” (because Michelle Obama got a Best Audiobook nomination and Jesse Welles’s “No Kings” is nominated for Best Americana Roots Recording), the Blaze wrote a pretty straightforward news post about the casting, and although it’s journalistic malpractice to quote any comments section to prove any larger societal point, when you get comments like…

“Is he still licensed to kill, or will he be an unarmed social worker with a handbook on de-escalation negotiation?”

On social media and comments sections and whatever that Dan Wootton show is, exhibit A for the case against Robert Pattinson’s masculinity is a 2023 Paris Fashion Week red carpet look: Pattinson in a fur jacket and a skirt. I don’t know, I think a man ought to defy conventions, go his own way, take some risks. Plus, the dude can rock a skirt. Don’t hate him because he’s beautiful.

So Pattinson is too fey to play Bond, and too famous from other iconic roles besides. This is, of course, the exact criticism leveled at Roger Moore in 1972 when he took the role over: that he was too lithe to compete with the hairy-shouldered thickness of Sean Connery, and that he was already too well-known for having played a similar role in The Saint. (And for having done it on television; “TV Star” was an epithet until right around The Sopranos.) Timothy Dalton was dinged for being too Shakespearean to take over for Moore. Pierce Brosnan had already played a parody of a Bond type on Remington Steele, so how could he compete with the gravitas of Dalton? Daniel Craig was criticized for being too beefy to take over for Brosnan. You can’t win.

The good news is that as much whining as there is about Pattinson and his lack of masculinity, there’s a lot less of it than there would have been even a few months ago. The “not man enough” chorus is singing, but they’re undeniably a little bit muted. Whether it has anything to do with Pete Hegseth and Markwayne Mullin and Sean Duffy and the rest of our government’s drag king grotesques of manliness publicly tripping on their dicks and compromising our basic security in the process I can’t say definitively, but it sure feels like it.

Among the many YouTube reaction videos, I came upon one that I am absolutely not going to watch, but whose title distills the argument down to its essence almost too perfectly.

There it is. I wanted someone else. The new thing is bad. The whole argument, like so many of the arguments against the evolution of masculinity, boils down to “things aren’t the way they were when I was younger, and I’m angry about it.” It’s the credo of the dull, the philosophy of the frightened, the logline of at least three Tim Allen sitcoms. Move on. Grow up. Let R-Patz cook.

I’ll meet you back here when we’re furious about Taylor Swift doing the theme song.

#6682. Kate Middleton’s Awkward Moment as a Fan Encourages Her to Break Protocol Ahead of the Earthshot Awards

The royal had to stick to her guns and wouldn’t back down.

The Princess of Wales, best known as Kate Middleton, had a slightly awkward moment on her tour of India this week (where she’s raising awareness for her husband Prince William’s Earthshot Prize Awards, which reward innovative environmental solutions). While greeting an excited crowd gathered in Mumbai, the Princess sadly had to disappoint a cluster of children who were hoping to get her autograph.

Speaking to one child who had a pen and paper in hand for the Princess, Catherine reportedly had to say, “Sorry, I can’t!” as to sign her name would be a severe breach of royal protocol.

None of the royal family are allowed to give out autographs, owing to fears that their signature could be replicated and forged, or that they’ll simply be sold on for monetary gain.

In recent years, they have become more amenable to people requesting selfies, which previously was not common practice.

It’s far from the first time the Princess has had to navigate this type of delicate situation with a member of the public; earlier this year while on a visit to Wales, she was also asked to sign her name and had to offer up a consolatory handshake instead.

The trip to India is Kate’s first international visit since her cancer diagnosis in 2024, and her attendance was somewhat unexpected. She has missed the last three Earthshot Prize ceremonies, but given she’s traveled all the way to Mumbai, it’s not a surprise that she showed up alongside William for this year’s event, wearing a stunning Anarkali by Indian designer Mahima Mahajan.

The 2026 Earthshot Prize ceremony will not air live but will be available to watch on Amazon Prime from November 30.

#6680. King Charles Had the Most Unexpected Response to Robert Pattinson Being Announced as James Bond

The royals have long been fans of the franchise.

King Charles and Queen Camilla had the best response to the long-awaited news that Twilight and Batman legend Robert Pattinson is set to become the new James Bond.

The royal family has long been associated with the iconic British spy series, with the King and Queen—along with Prince William and Catherine, Princess of Wales—attending the No Time to Die premiere at London’s Royal Albert Hall in September 2021 (the last film that was released in the long-running franchise).

In response to the official James Bond X account sharing the news about Pattinson, the royal family's account (which acts as a news source for the King and Queen), re-shared the post along with the comment: “Welcome to His Majesty’s Secret Service, Mr Bond.”

So far, the quip has been liked over 250,000 times.

Prior to the 2021 premiere, the King had attended plenty of other screenings of the Bond series—dating as far back as 1981 for the launch of For Your Eyes Only, which had Roger Moore starring as the fictional MI6 agent.

The late Princess Diana and Princess Margaret also turned out for that particular premiere.

Commenting on his major career leap, Robert Pattinson said in a statement, “James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honor of a lifetime.”

He continued, “I’m deeply grateful to Denis, Amy, David, Tanya, and everyone at Amazon MGM Studios for their belief in me. To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement. I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

Filming is expected to begin early next year, with an unspecified release date slated for sometime in 2028.

It will be the first James Bond movie produced by Amazon MGM, after the Broccoli family unexpectedly sold the rights in 2025.

#6677. From Dark Chocolate to Milky Mocha, These Brown Nail Polishes Are So Good for Any Fall Manicure

These make a rich manicure so easy to DIY.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

To me, November means a few things: Friendsgiving feasts, Black Friday shopping, and most importantly for us manicure devotees, brown nail polish season. It’s the time of year when I start reaching for richer hues, and brown is one of my favorite shades to wear this time of year.

So, whether you’re on your DIY manicure journey or want to bring your own bottle to your next nail appointment, I rounded up the best brown nail polish shades to try this fall and winter.

If you're after a true deep espresso shade, Essie's Odd Squad is the perfect brown polish. It's a dark chocolate brown that’s equal parts moody and rich, and it looks universally flattering on any skin tone.

For a slightly lighter and creamier brown hue, this OPI shade is more like a milky coffee or a warm cup of hot cocoa. It's a softer take on brown that's still plenty cozy for fall.

If you’re someone who doesn’t have the patience or time to sit around waiting for your nails to dry (same), a quick-drying polish is the way to go. This formula helps cut down on drying time, so you can get back to your day sooner and spend less time worrying about accidentally smudging your fresh manicure.

A gel polish is a great way to get more mileage out of your favorite brown shade. This OPI formula cures under a UV or LED lamp, leaving you with a high-shine finish and salon-quality longevity right at home. And the entire bottle is cheaper than the price of one salon manicure!

If you're going for a darker manicure, a long-wearing formula is especially ideal since chips tend to be more noticeable against deeper shades. Sally Hansen's Xtreme Wear is a great option, with a durable finish designed to help your manicure last longer. Plus, it’s less than $3 a bottle!

Cat eye and shimmery polishes are trending for winter, and this formula from Zoya in the shade Thatcher gives you the iridescent finish—no magnets or curing necessary.

#6676. How to Watch Every Movie in the Marvel Cinematic Universe

It’s easy. You only need time. And a Disney+ subscription.

Suddenly, we’re excited about Marvel again. Hitting theaters on December 18 is Avengers: Doomsday, the long-awaited next Avengers sequel. You may have heard that Robert Downey Jr. is back, only now in the role of the villainous Doctor Doom instead of Iron Man. As the man himself said back at San Diego Comic-Con in July 2024: “New mask, same task.” Whatever that means. (Also, if you think you ever heard of something called Avengers: The Kang Dynasty, no, you didn’t.)

Anyway, it’s been seven years since we last saw a new Avengers movie. Back then, you might have Googled something like, “How to watch every Marvel movie.” I have a gut feeling that’s what brought you here now. As Doomsday awakens dormant feelings about superheroes and multiverses, you probably need a guide to help you revisit all the best Marvel films or catch up on the ones you’ve missed. Lucky for you, it is easy to catch up. You just need time and a Disney+ subscription. If you start now, you’ll roll credits on The Fantastic Four: First Steps by December 17.

Here’s how to watch every Marvel movie before Avengers: Doomsday. Check back a year from now, when we’re doing it all over again for Avengers: Secret Wars.

Here’s the good news: Except for Spider-Man: Brand New Day, which came out this summer and is now on digital, every Marvel Cinematic Universe movie is available on Disney+. Every. Single. One.

Iron Man? Yes. Infinity War? Yup. Captain America: The Winter Soldier? Hell yeah. Guardians of the Galaxy? All three, baby! Ant-Man and the Wasp: Quantumania? Maybe skip that one. And since Disney acquired Fox in 2018, you’ll also find all the X-Men, Deadpool, and the two Fantastic Four movies from the mid-2000s.

There’s also plenty of Marvel television. Not just streaming shows like WandaVision, The Falcon and the Winter Soldier, Ms. Marvel, and the recent VisionQuest, but also network shows like Agents of S.H.I.E.L.D. and Agent Carter, as well as the Netflix Defenders-verse. If you’re really hardcore, you can also watch the handful of “One Shot” short films that were originally released on several Blu-rays.

The only “Marvel movies” you can’t watch on Disney+ are (most) of the Sony-produced movies. These include Morbius, Madame Web, Kraven the Hunter, and the Venom trilogy. But because distribution rights are funny, the Tobey Maguire and Andrew Garfield Spider-Man movies are on Disney+, along with Spider-Man: Across the Spider-Verse as I write this.

I hear you. Short of time-traveling back to 2000 and watching every movie as they come out, it feels impossible to “catch up” with all of it now. Especially since Avengers: Doomsday is once again dealing with the multiverse, which means even “non-canon” movies (like, say, the Blade or Punisher movies) might get a shout-out.

Luckily, Marvel knows your pain. On Disney+, a new “Countdown to Avengers: Doomsday” sub-page highlights the most important movies. While there are glaring omissions, watching only the highlighted movies can bump just about anyone up to speed. Even people who say they’ve “never seen one of these things.”

For your convenience, I’ve listed below EVERY movie from the Fox X-Men universe, the Sony Spider-Man universes, and the Marvel Cinematic Universe, in order of theatrical release. This is the only correct “viewing order.” Do not listen to YouTubers who insist on some “timeline order” nonsense. You can be an adult and watch these things exactly how they were produced and released. The movies Marvel deems essential for Doomsday prep are listed in bold, with the trailers attached.

Blade is rightfully credited with kickstarting the 21st-century superhero boom, but the X-Men films set the tone as must-see summer blockbusters. The first film in Fox’s X-Men series introduced audiences to mutants, with Hugh Jackman as Wolverine, the late Patrick Stewart as Charles Xavier, and Ian McKellen as radical extremist Magneto, who leads his Brotherhood in a fight for equal rights. Famke Janssen and James Marsden, who also starred as Jean Grey and Cyclops, respectively, will return in Doomsday, but Halle Berry’s Storm is sitting it out.

The battle for mutant liberation continues when Colonel Stryker (Brian Cox) kidnaps Xavier, forcing the X-Men to align with the Brotherhood of Mutants. In this better-than-you-remember sequel, mutantkind stands side by side for the sake of their survival. Though X2 predates shared universes, its scope, scale, and political intrigue make it one of the greatest and most underrated sequels of all time.

“I am Iron Man.” The resurrection of Robert Downey Jr.’s career blasted off to the stratosphere, along with the Marvel Cinematic Universe itself, in a movie that officially kicked off the MCU. Kidnapped by terrorists and forced into a cave with scraps, playboy futurist Tony Stark (Downey Jr.) escapes as the armored superhero Iron Man and returns home to find that his enemies are far closer to him than he suspects. When the smoke clears, a shadowy figure named Nick Fury (Samuel L. Jackson) informs him that the universe is way bigger than he knows.

Once, Chris Evans was the Human Torch. Suddenly, he wasn’t. As the Marvel Cinematic Universe picked up steam, the sweltering summer of 2011 saw Captain America: The First Avenger. Set in World War II, the first Marvel “prequel” stars Evans as the brave shrimp of Brooklyn, Steve Rogers, who becomes the first to endure the experimental “Super Soldier” program. As Captain America, Rogers takes the fight to Red Skull, whose dark ambition lies beyond the Third Reich.

There once was an idea: To bring together a remarkable group of cinematic superheroes to see if they can make cinematic history. In 2012, Marvel did just that. The first crossover Avengers movie saw the growing roster of Marvel’s cinematic superheroes team up for the very first time. Though Marvel movies have only gotten bigger and even better since, we cannot deny the seismic impact of The Avengers.

Captain America: The Winter Soldier (2014)

After The Avengers planted the seeds for Thanos, all of Marvel’s storytelling shifted to an adaptation of the 1991 comic crossover The Infinity Gauntlet. In 2018, Marvel released Avengers: Infinity War, the third Avengers movie that forever changed the MCU’s status quo. With Josh Brolin as Thanos, the Marvel Universe’s many heroes band together to stave off the greatest threat anyone had ever seen. While audiences knew another movie was around the corner, its shock ending dared to ask the unthinkable: What happens when heroes lose?

It all came down to this. In 2019, on the same weekend Game of Thrones staged its own final battle, Avengers: Endgame changed the trajectory of the Marvel Universe once more. The fourth Avengers film sees the heroes dust off for a rematch against Thanos. But before they can do that, they must travel back in time through their own history to collect the Infinity Stones if they want to stand a chance.

In September 2026, in preparation for Doomsday, Marvel released an extended cut of Avengers: Endgame, titled Encore. It comes with a few extra minutes of scenes that frankly ruin the grace of Endgame in its original form, but if you really want to see clearer connective tissue to Doomsday, check out Encore.

In the 1970s, one of the most popular Marvel Comics titles wasn’t The Amazing Spider-Man, Uncanny X-Men, or The Avengers. It was Master of Kung Fu, starring an ass-kicker named Shang-Chi. He was a master martial artist and the son of a supervillain (Fu Manchu in the comics) who, over time, works as a secret agent for MI6. He was created by Steve Englehart and Jim Starlin (who also created Thanos) in the spirit of Bruce Lee and the TV show Kung Fu.

After decades of obscurity, the box-office success of Crazy Rich Asians in 2018 inspired Marvel to dig into the longboxes and put the spotlight back on Shang-Chi. In 2021, Simu Liu brought him to life in Shang-Chi and the Legend of the Ten Rings, an origin movie that introduces Shang-Chi for the 21st century. Hong Kong screen legend Tony Leung (In the Mood for Love) costars as Wenwu, Shang-Chi’s father, who runs the terrorist group the Ten Rings—named after Wenwu’s mysterious alien weapons—which kidnapped Tony Stark in the first Iron Man.

Shang-Chi’s use of the Ten Rings has yet-unknown ties to the wider Marvel Universe. After five years, we may finally have some answers in Avengers: Doomsday.

After Spider-Man was publicly unmasked as Peter Parker at the end of Spider-Man: Far From Home, the teenage superhero turns to Sorcerer Supreme Doctor Strange (Benedict Cumberbatch) for help. But when the magic goes wrong, the multiverse cracks open, and villains from other realities—as in, other Spider-Man franchises—seep through the cracks. Tom Holland’s Spider-Man teams up with two veteran Spideys, played by Tobey Maguire and Andrew Garfield (remember them?), to put the universe back in one piece.

Doctor Strange saved the universe in Avengers: Endgame and restored the multiverse in Spider-Man: No Way Home. But can he save himself? In this direct sequel to 2016’s Doctor Strange and set after both Spider-Man: No Way Home and the Disney+ series WandaVision, Stephen must help protect a young girl, America Chavez (Xochitl Gomez), from a vengeful Wanda (Elizabeth Olsen), who wants to use America’s unique powers to reunite with her own lost family.

Originally billed as Marvel’s first horror movie (its very title alluding to Lovecraft’s At the Mountains of Madness), creative reshuffling led to Evil Dead and Spider-Man director Sam Raimi helming the movie. This explains the movie’s uniquely darker tone than one might expect from a typical Marvel tentpole. Cameos in this film tie directly to some of Marvel’s other far corners, like TV shows What If...? and Inhumans, as well as the Fox X-Men movies.

Werewolf by Night (2022), a “Marvel Studios Special Presentation” on Disney+

The real-life passing of Chadwick Boseman in 2020 left a huge hole in everyone’s hearts—and Marvel in a difficult place. Without its star, writer/director Ryan Coogler redesigned his plans for the sequel to his Earth-shattering 2018 hit.

Taking inspiration from Reginald Hudlin’s run in the comics, Black Panther: Wakanda Forever sets the stage for a new Black Panther: Shuri, T’Challa’s genius sister, played by Letitia Wright. But before Shuri can take up her brother’s mantle, she must first learn how heavy crowns are when another kingdom emerges from the depths and threatens Wakanda. The sequel introduces Namor (Tenoch Huerta), from the underwater world Talokan (originally Atlantis in the comics). The two will play a larger role in Avengers: Doomsday.

The Guardians of the Galaxy Holiday Special (2022), a “Marvel Studios Special Presentation” on Disney+

After Hugh Jackman retired from playing Wolverine in the 2017 masterpiece Logan, Marvel and Ryan Reynolds shelled out a truckload of money to get him back and into some yellow spandex at last. The third movie in the Deadpool series, Deadpool & Wolverine, marked the MCU debut of Ryan Reynolds’ scrotum-faced Deadpool, whose dream of joining the Avengers clashes with the collapse of his own reality. More cameos, callbacks, and cusses fill the movie’s two-hour runtime.

While Jackman and Channing Tatum—who was once tapped to play the X-Men member Gambit, which Deadpool & Wolverine finally made a reality—will return in Doomsday, it’s unknown whether Reynolds will too. But if his surprise appearance at Comic-Con means anything, then...

When Steve Rogers gave Sam Wilson (Anthony Mackie) the shield of Captain America in Endgame, Sam wasn’t ready to take up the responsibility so soon. He still had his own things to work out. After the Disney+ series The Falcon and the Winter Soldier, Sam now flies the skies as Captain America, with the 2025 film Captain America: Brave New World marking the start of his era as the Star-Spangled Man. In addition to being the fourth Captain America movie, the movie revisits the events of 2008’s The Incredible Hulk and 2021’s Eternals.

The Marvel franchise was built on a studio elevating comic book benchwarmers into A-listers. That tradition was upheld in the 2025 movie Thunderbolts*. Based on a Marvel Comics series where supervillains form a team, the big-screen version recruits several “forgotten” antiheroes from different corners of the MCU. Together, they form a new team that must stave off their own assassination and save the world—on their terms. You can watch the movie cold, but know that many of these characters come from movies like Captain America: The Winter Soldier, Black Widow, Ant-Man and the Wasp, and the TV show The Falcon and the Winter Soldier.

And now, we step into a whole ‘nother world. After multiple attempts at a big-screen Fantastic Four, the Marvel Cinematic Universe launched its own take on the First Family in The Fantastic Four: First Steps. Set on a different Earth in 1964, the Fantastic Four rise to the occasion when the planet is threatened by Galactus.

Look. Don’t put yourself through that. There are too many. If you insist on watching any of them, just watch Loki. It has two seasons and just 12 episodes total, so you can knock it out in one weekend. Loki takes place after Avengers: Endgame (technically after just The Avengers, but how and why is too long to explain), and of all the shows, it bears the strongest ties to Doomsday. Talk about a glorious purpose.

#6675. “Sexy, Weirdo Bond”: Why Women Love Robert Pattinson’s Casting as 007

It feels like we might finally get a more vulnerable, less sexist James Bond.

Whether you were waiting with bated breath or not, the years-long wait to find out who the new James Bond is over, with the announcement that everyone’s fave Gen Z Robert Pattinson is set to take the reins.

In many ways, his casting is totally unsurprising—he is, seemingly, Hollywood’s only leading man right now, appearing in The Drama, The Odyssey, and Primetime this year alone (with Dune: Part Three still to come), on top of being literal Batman. And yet, at the same time, Rob as Bond feels like a break with tradition. I mean, he’s still a handsome straight white guy — but he’s not your typical alpha male.

There’s a silliness, darkness, and freakiness (complimentary) to Pattinson and the characters he’s played. With projects famously traversing both indie flicks and major blockbusters, he tends to appear as more fuckboy, weirdo, or Bond villain than the emotionally distant, hunky womanizer typically synonymous with 007. And women in particular seem to be into it.

I’m not just talking about women who grew up crushing on Cedric Diggory and Edward Cullen, either. Not to assign too much weight to the James Bond franchise, but Pattinson’s casting comes at a time when we’re grappling with what masculinity means today. In an era when misogynistic manosphere figureheads are as prevalent on our screens as Hollywood stars, there’s less appetite for clichéd portrayals of the archetypal alpha Bond guy, who’s super jacked, can’t express his emotions, and treats women like disposable objects.

For heterosexual women, this extends to our romantic lives, too. A new survey by female-led dating app Chyrpe found that over half of women wouldn’t actively look for an “alpha male” in a partner, with 70 percent saying they’d rather date a supportive, emotionally available man who’s happy to let them take the lead (Bond as a sub?!).

“The alpha male is traditionally understood as a man who is dominant, assertive, and accustomed to being in control,” says Gigi Engle, relationship expert for Chyrpe. “There’s an assumption that he’s emotionally self-sufficient, sexually dominant, and rarely, if ever, vulnerable. Psychologically, this represents a pretty narrow and restrictive understanding of masculinity.”

Despite what the manosphere would have you believe, this performance of masculinity doesn’t work for anyone involved. “When men feel they have to constantly be this ‘dominant’ character, there’s often very little room for emotional openness or genuine partnership,” continues Engle. “Women may simply be getting tired of having to accommodate someone else’s need to feel powerful. It’s boring, frankly.” In fact, the same Chyrpe survey found that 33 percent of men feel pressured by social media to embody the alpha male stereotype, while 30 percent say male friends ridicule them if they don’t take on an alpha role in their relationship.

There’s an opportunity, then, for macho franchises like James Bond to change things. “There’s a growing thirst for a masculinity that feels more human and less traditional,” says Engle. “For decades, popular culture has sold us the idea that an attractive man is someone who is powerful, emotionally restrained, physically strong, and always in control. But those expectations are starting to feel dated and lame. We’re becoming more interested in men who can hold complexity. We want guys who are comfortable with women who don’t need them to take charge.”

It feels like in Pattinson’s hands, we might see 007’s softer, nerdier, more emotional side—which could be a good thing for both men and women.

At least, that’s what a bunch of women I spoke to are hoping for. “He’s a James Bond you can see shooting the bad guys and also snuggling up in front of the telly watching Bridget Jones—and he’s kinda awkward,” says Clare.

Another Claire tells me: “I just cannot imagine him enthusiastically doing some of the cringier, misogynistic lines or scenes with female costars. I think he’ll be able to bring charisma, equally he doesn’t try to overpower his female costars. He holds his own in a scene without it ever needing to take anything away from the people he’s sharing that scene with.”

Nicole says a “different portrayal of masculinity would be nice,” but if we don’t get that, “he should be bisexual at least.”

Others, including Engle, are excited by the darkness Pattinson might bring to Bond. “He has an unconventional screen presence, to say the least,” she says. “There’s an introspective, slightly unpredictable quality to him that feels different from the traditionally hyper-masculine Bond we’ve become accustomed to. I’d love to see a Bond who retains the confidence, danger, and sexual charisma of the character but is allowed to have real emotions.”

Another woman I spoke to, Kitty, feels similarly: “First of all, [Pattinson] is my all-time crush, but I’m excited because I wonder if they’ll take the opportunity to make him darker and explore the theme of him being an alcoholic and reclusive. Similar to how he made Batman emo, will they make Bond more moody? Because so many people have explored how fucked up [Bond] actually is as a person, it would be cool to bring it to the front.”

Or, as Elise sums it up: “Let’s fucking go for sexy, weirdo Bond.”

Until the first film drops in 2028, we’ll have no idea how Pattinson will play Bond—but there’s no harm in projecting our emotionally intelligent fantasies onto him in the meantime.

#6673. A Guide to the Best Canada Goose Jackets

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

High performance or high fashion? There’s a pick for each.

Since 1957, Canada Goose has been crushing the luxury outerwear game. And if its decades of history tell you one thing, it’s that Canada Goose isn’t going anywhere anytime soon. In fact, one of the best decisions that you can make is investing in a piece or two (if you haven’t already) because it’s a purchase that’s going to pay off. The high-quality designs are durable, practical, and stand at a timeless intersection where fashion meets function.

The biggest issue we have with Canada Goose is that there are too many options to choose from. You’re probably already aware that the brand has more than just its hefty parkas. Sure, it’s got those too, of course, but we also love its bombers, flannels, and fleeces. Canada Goose brand has even been venturing into the high-fashion space, with its creative director Haider Ackermann debuting collaborations with the NBA’s Shai Gilgeous-Alexander, CNCPTS, and KidSuper, among others.To help you find the right fit for you, we did a full breakdown for you. Below, find our official guide to the six best Canada Goose jackets. You’re not going to regret getting any of these.

This bomber jacket has some history to it. Originally designed over 25 years ago, this iteration of the jacket fuses modern functionality with classic bomber detailing, like a double placket, pen pocket, and ribbed waist. The archive-inspired satin gives it a glossy finish, but it’s still subtle enough that it will go with pretty much just about anything. Wear it with some black jeans, combat or Chelsea boots, and you’ve got a badass fit.

Every man should own a bomber jacket, so if for some reason the Seguin Aviator didn’t resonate with you, maybe this one will. Inspired by the brand’s 2004 Air Force parka, this bomber adds a warm fleece for insulation—as well as style points. With five pockets (two welt pockets at the chest, two lower pockets with coin-snaps, one zippered sleeve pocket), it masters practicality.

You can never go wrong with a traditional puffer. It’s actually more of a prerequisite than anything else, especially if you live somewhere with an actual winter. Canada Goose’s puffer is top-of-the-line, as it’s ultra-lightweight, super soft, and durable. So many puffer jackets weigh you down, but this one doesn’t. Its features are also endless: There are backpack straps, recessed rib-knit cuffs that add comfort and lock in heat, interior drawcord to customize fit, two exterior fleece-lined handwarmer pockets with zipper closures, three interior pockets, two mesh drop-in pockets, one security pocket—and more.

If you want a streamlined parka that doesn’t have too much going on on the outside—but still has a ton of cool features inside—the Langford is the one for you. It has a sleek silhouette that’s slightly longer than a puffer, and it’s even warmer than a puffer, too. With 625 Fill Power down inside, it can protect you in temperatures down to -13°F, and it will look great while doing it. It also has an adjustable down-filled hood, lined chin guard, two-way zipper underneath snap closures, and fleece-lined upper hand pockets so you can really keep the cold out and turn up the warmth.

The Expedition Parka is Canada Goose’s original—and most iconic—extreme-weather jacket. Initially developed for scientists working in Antarctica, this jacket is made to keep you warm in the most extreme of environments. It does that—and then some—with its elasticized snow skirt for added protection, interior drawcord at the waist to customize fit and retain warmth, and double-entry pockets for easy access. We could elaborate even more here, but you can just check out our love letter to the iconic parka for all the details.

But this version is even cooler than the original: It’s part of the Snow Goose line, which is basically creative director Haider Ackermann’s passion project. It features some more fashion-forward designs, stuff that’s harder to wear. However, this version of the Expedition Parka keeps the classic shape, but it comes in two seasonal colors, so you’ll stand out from everyone else wearing the same model, but won’t feel like you’re on the runway. It’s a win-win.

We know this puffer jacket looks like a sweater, but that’s kind of the point: It has the fun design of a winter sweater and the warmth of a down jacket—and when you add in the soft feel of the exterior from the wool-alpaca blend, you’re getting a lot of good stuff. It doesn’t look too bulky, but it can keep you warm down to -4°F, if you can believe it. It’s a good alternative to the more classic polyester or nylon puffer jackets; just be careful about taking it out in heavy rain or snow, and keep in mind that the exterior material requires more delicate handling than the usual puffer jacket material.

#6672. Is There a Right Way to Respond to a Grammy Snub?

Artists like John Summit and MUNA have reacted to being shut out by the Recording Academy. But is there a correct way to approach it?

Not to go all “question for the culture” on everyone, but this year’s Grammy nominations have been on my mind for too long for me not to ask: Is there really a “right” way to acknowledge being snubbed by the Academy?

While the Recording Academy granted cultural aficionados the pleasant surprise of recognizing longtime underdogs like Raye, Remi Wolf, Tinashe, and Underscores, some names were noticeably absent from this year’s nominations list—and they had something to say about it.

For starters, EDM giant John Summit made headlines for his unfiltered reaction to being left out of the Dance and Electronic categories.

“never submitting to the grammys again unless shit changes, it’s all a fucking joke,” he wrote, criticizing the Academy for only recognizing “music industry fueled pop stars.”

After receiving backlash from online bystanders—including Charli xcx, who he previously requested to hop on a track in 2024—he walked back his claims, writing, “i realized i didn’t do a good job of making my point earlier. my intent was not to drag other artists down and i seriously congratulate everyone who was nominated. they fully deserve the recognition. i just think there should be an extra category for more dance music djs & producers.”

John isn’t wrong to point out the Academy’s standards in certain categories, but his delivery was a bit aggressive. Especially after he seemingly diminished fans’ opinions and deemed them “lil teeny bopper pop stan accounts with zero real world life experience.” It’s…a choice to say that about the people who move culture forward.

Naturally, his reaction got me thinking about the unspoken etiquette of addressing a snub. It’s valid to be upset over not being recognized by your peers, as well as seasoned vets in the biz. But you don’t want to ruin your chances for redemption, either.

Take Noah Kahan, for example. He reassured his fans that he’s “done everything I’ve ever needed to do in my career because of YOUR support” ahead of the nominations, only to be shut out in the core categories: Record of the Year, Song of the Year, and Album of the Year. Though his album The Great Divide still earned a nod in the Best Contemporary Folk Album category, he acknowledged his snub with three words: “Life goes on.” Simple, yet effective!

Another artist who didn’t land a nod was pop princess and Cosmo cover girl Kim Petras, who made history as the first openly transgender woman to win a Grammy back in 2023. Her career took a tumultuous turn when she signed a major-label deal after riding the success of her award-winning track with Sam Smith, “Unholy.”

Years into her deal, she publicly accused her label of gatekeeping funds and resources, and pleaded to be released from her contract. She ultimately returned to her independent roots for her latest record, Detour, funding most of the studio sessions, visuals, and live performances herself. When the album didn’t earn a nod, fans claimed she was “snubbed,” since her hard work deserved recognition.

“I wouldn’t say so … I just left the industry tbh and I made the music to make the music cause I love it so much,” she wrote in response, claiming that her “nomination days [are] over” as she roots for Tinashe and Underscores.

The self-proclaimed “Greatest Band in the World,” MUNA, also started gaining momentum since trading a larger label for Phoebe Bridgers’ independent Saddest Factory Records in 2022. Their latest album, Dancing on the Wall, drew massive crowds at festivals like Lollapalooza and All Things Go last summer. The Academy has yet to acknowledge them, despite continued success.

The band’s lead singer, Katie Gavin, released a short video addressing fans after nominations dropped, saying, “I wasn’t expecting it, and I understand it. And it’s tough for a band like us because we make pop music, but we’re not as big as other pop artists.”

She added that they attempted to submit for alternative categories, but they ultimately didn’t make the cut because of their inherent pop sound. In the end, she kept things candid.

To put it plainly, there isn’t a definitive right or wrong way to go about being slept on. If artists decide to publicly address their snubs in the future, they should grab their pens and papers and take notes from Katie’s final sentiment.

“I’m really proud of all the people that did get nominated. There was so much amazing music this year,” she shared. “I hate being disappointed, but I’m not giving up hope.”

#6669. Why Do Fans Hate to See an Up-and-Coming Male Actor With a “Normie” GF? Unpacking This Parasocial Phenomenon

From Belmont Cameli to Christopher Briney, fans are fully losing the plot.

Amid breakup rumors (which were almost certainly manufactured), Belmont Cameli stepped out with his TV writer girlfriend Raina Morris for a West Hollywood date night on November 10. The two were together before Off Campus premiered, catapulting Belmont into the public eye, but his fans remain convinced that (1) Belmont is meant to be with his costar Ella Bright and (2) Raina must have an ulterior motive in dating him—that motive, of course, being tangential fame.

Here are a few select comments from Just Jared’s post about the outing on Instagram, to give you an idea of what I’m talking about:

As a reminder: This all comes a couple of months after Raina was forced to release a statement asking Off Campus fans to stop harassing her family. “If you are a fan of my boyfriend: Yay!” she started her message, adding, “Please stop messaging my family members. They are so confused. Write in your diary and then go on a walk instead! Love u.”

Raina is not the only non-actor girlfriend of a rising star to experience this kind of cruel harassment. Similarly, when it came to light that Heated Rivalry star Hudson Williams was not dating his costar Connor Storie in real life (as much as fans wanted that to be true) but was instead dating tattoo artist Katelyn Rose Larson (and had been for years), some people felt a certain type of way.

Hudson first introduced fans to his girlfriend with an Instagram Story post on Valentine’s Day 2026 (two months after Deuxmoi revealed her identity to the public), noting that Katelyn has been with him “since my 2000 gold Mazda Protégé smoked and squealed and I had no job.” But still, when Katelyn joined Hudson on the red carpet at an Oscars after-party the next month, some fans weren’t shy about making their disapproval known...and they’ve been keeping it up ever since.

Again, I present a few comments from a Deuxmoi post showing the two hugging before Heated Rivalry season 2 filming kicked off back in August:

A month after those paparazzi shots of their hug went viral, Katelyn took to her Instagram Stories to write, “Obviously a lot of you have found the studio because of a certain show and that’s more than okay. This is where I draw a very hard line: booking an appointment with me or anyone else purely because of the connection to the show crosses a huge territory.”

She added, “Showing up to your appointment and asking my coworkers any sort of questions about myself or about Hudson is hugely uncomfortable for everyone.”

And of course, we can’t have this conversation without mentioning Christopher Briney’s girlfriend, fellow actor Isabel Machado, who he started dating in college and who has faced no shortage of harassment from The Summer I Turned Pretty fans...much of it due to the fact that she kiiiiinda looks like his onscreen love interest, played by Lola Tung.

I present to you: select comments from a Deuxmoi post announcing Chris and Isabel’s engagement (though that rumor turned out to be totally false).

And while Isabel has never released a statement directly calling out fans, she seemed to respond to harassment in her own subtle way with an August 2025 Instagram post reminding everyone that she’s a “person with feelings.”

So...why is it that fans hate to see an up-and-coming male actor with a less-famous girlfriend? For starters, I don’t think it’s a coincidence that all three of these men star in beloved TV romances...or that their fandoms all want them to be with their onscreen love interests IRL.

But Ella Bright and Belmont Cameli are not actually Hannah Wells and Garrett Graham. And while the actors seem to have great chemistry both onscreen and offscreen, fans must remember that they’re actors for a reason: They’re good at fooling audiences. And dating someone other than their costar doesn’t mean they’re fooling themselves.

These three rising stars have also all been in relationships since before their breakout roles, so to accuse their partners of social climbing seems not only rude, but straight-up false. While they may not have millions of followers, these supposed “normie” girlfriends are all successful in their own right, and it’s been made clear that none of them are trying to steal the spotlight.

“She’s not trying to be out there or be perceived,” Hudson said of Katelyn to ETalk Canada back in March. “She’s always like, ‘I don’t want to be perceived!’”

While Belmont doesn’t talk about Raina directly, he did address the “real venom” of “fandom these days,” telling The Hollywood Reporter, “You can say whatever you want about me or people in my life, but it’s not going to affect me because I’m fully aware of what my reality looks like and feels like.”

Chris also expressed a similar sentiment to Elite Daily when talking about his relationship with Isabel, saying, “People have definitely crossed lines and stepped on personal boundaries...I’ve learned to separate myself from social media and understand that I have my own life. If people have opinions about it, all right, that’s fine.”

Still, it seems fans cannot stop hurling accusations and spreading breakup rumors whenever they’re reminded that Conrad Fisher is not their boyfriend...or Belly Conklin’s boyfriend...or even a real person. Yes, these so-called nepo girlfriends have gained more followers than they may have if their boyfriends were still struggling actors. But chances are, they would have stuck with their men even if they never landed their breakout roles (and are still with them despite what may be totally unwanted attention).

Point being: You can ‘ship your fave fictional relationship all you want. You can even think, “Why not me?” when you see your fave TV heartthrob with someone else. But when you come for the very real people in their lives, you’ve done more than lose the plot; you’ve crossed into parasocial behavior.

To quote Hudson: “RPFing gotta stop, y’all.”

#6667. These Holiday Perfumes Smell Like the Most Wonderful Time of the Year

‘Tis the season... to smell really freaking good.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Call me Buddy the Elf, but IMO, there’s no such thing as getting too in the holiday spirit. I’ve been known to display multiple Christmas trees, I love putting together holiday cards, and cooking Christmas dinner for friends and family is one of my great joys in life. And of course, all of this is happening while I’m blasting Mariah Carey and all my other favorite holiday songs.

But don’t think I’ve forgotten about the beauty side of things! As a faithful beauty editor, I also get excited to change up my fragrance this time of year and embrace some holiday scents. Do I want to smell just like a Christmas tree? No, not really. But I do want to spritz something that’ll help get me in the holiday spirit. Think about all the best scents of the season—gingerbread, pine, cinnamon spice, vanilla... These make for perfect holiday perfumes. So, in the name of very important holiday beauty research, I’ve rounded up the best festive fragrances worth adding to your rotation this season. From cozy gourmand perfumes to fresh woodsy notes and everything in between, one spritz of any of these will instantly get you in the holiday spirit.

Nest’s famous holiday candle is a staple in my home, and now, I can wear the scent on my skin too. It bottles up that festive feeling with fir balsam, winter cranberry, and grapefruit for a perfume that’s equal parts fresh, fruity, and woodsy. Think wandering through a Christmas tree farm with a little tart cranberry cocktail. It’s festive without smelling like you’re hiding pine branches up your sleeves.

Holiday parties call for a little spiked eggnog, and Dior Spice Blend brings that same boozy, spicy energy to your fragrance wardrobe. Rich rum notes, fiery black pepper, and zesty ginger blend together for a warm, intoxicating, and spirited scent. I love that it’s bold, sophisticated, and much less sugary than a typical holiday gourmand.

You don’t actually have to know how to ski to appreciate a good après ski moment (bring on the hot chocolate and jacuzzi, please). Ellis Brooklyn’s wintry scent captures that exact winter getaway feeling, thanks to seasonal notes of juniper berries, cardamom, soft suede, and musk. It’s woodsy, a little romantic, and pretty much begging to be worn at a chic mountain lodge.

Hot chocolate is a holiday must-have for me, but let’s be real: The best part is the mountain of whipped cream on top. Philosophy Fresh Cream bottles up that sweet, fluffy deliciousness with notes of whipped cream, tonka bean, heliotrope, and sugar. It’s soft, milky, and comforting without any heaviness or spice. And at under $60, it’s a pretty sweet addition to your holiday fragrance wardrobe.

Dreaming of a white Christmas? Future Society Snow Beginnings can make that happen. It smells like the magic of a fresh snowfall, but with a surprisingly fruity, floral twist. Cool Japanese mint and sparkling pink grapefruit deliver that feeling of crisp winter air, while lychee, crystallized rose, and sake add a sweet warmth. It’s a winter wonderland in a bottle, no freezing temperatures or shoveling required.

Who says you have to spend a fortune to smell festive? Dove’s Holiday Treats Frosted Plum Berry Body Mist delivers a juicy, fruity take on holiday fragrance for just about the same price as your seasonal latte. The sweet, bright, and surprising plum feels playful and is a refreshing departure from the usual dessert-heavy holiday scents. Plus, it’s easy to layer with your favorite body lotion, scrub, or perfume for a little extra festive flair.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo who covers all things beauty, including fragrance.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.
