#6640. Office Hours: Saie’s Laney Crowell on the Beauty of Doing Good

For Laney Crowell, “feel good, do good” is not just the core philosophy of her beauty brand, Saie, but how she lives her life. “So much of beauty is not only who you are on the inside, but the actions that you take,” she says, emphasizing “the importance of taking action, especially in things that you are excited and passionate about.”

The driving force behind Saie was Crowell’s growing desire to make beauty better by creating formulas that are not just safe for your skin but also good for it. She wanted to create products that spoke to her as a self-professed “expert consumer.” “I’ve always struggled with really sensitive, acne-prone skin,” she says. “I had an aha moment where I realized that my makeup was what was making me break out. I started a blog [called The Moment] to have the conversation. It was niche, but at the same time, I was one of the only people having this conversation.” Saie was born from this community building seven years ago.

The brand has steadily expanded since then, without ever straying from its mission. Each new product follows the same principles: It’s formulated to nourish the skin, is noncomedogenic, and is clinically tested and dermatologist tested. “When you try Saie, you immediately feel the textures and how hydrating they are. Then as you continue to use them, you realize how happy your skin is. You’re really seeing benefits over time.”

Saie’s innovations, however, haven’t come without growing pains. Working with a long list of ingredients to avoid, including silicones, the team has found that developing long-lasting products takes a long time. “I only ever want to launch something if it’s perfect,” Crowell says. Take its City Set setting spray, for example. When it debuted in December 2025, no setting spray claimed 16-hour wear.

“I make beauty products for women who get ready in five minutes, just like me,” says Crowell, who is gearing up to solve another customer request. Saie’s next launch, CityStick, took two years to perfect and is set to debut later this year. “I’m always on the go, so I wanted something I could throw in my bag, swipe on for a bit of radiance, and know I was also doing something good for my skin. That’s CityStick—my makeup and skin care in one quick step.” It’s 50% makeup, 50% skin care; expect the sheer, soft-focus, radiant coverage of a skin tint with the benefits of nourishing skin care.

Helming a cosmetics brand—not to mention pioneering an entire category—requires vision and tenacity, two things Crowell has in equal measure. The last seven years have shown her just how resilient she is. “The most important attribute of a founder and entrepreneur is the ability to keep going,” she says. The other thing she’s learned? How much she loves to improve. “I am always thinking about how I, the brand, and everything that we do get 1% better every day.”

Beyond formulating, leading, and championing a host of climate initiatives (the brand has Planet Beautiful, an annual sustainability and plastic-recovery campaign in partnership with Sephora and RePurpose Global), Crowell is also a mother of two. She’s as passionate about Saie’s commitment to our planet (read: making systemic change regarding the billions of units of plastic the beauty industry generates each year) as she is about showing up for her two little girls.

“My weekdays are crazy, but I love it,” she says with a laugh. Days at her home upstate start with a 6:30 a.m. matcha (Rocky’s ceremonial-grade matcha with steamed oat milk and simple syrup she makes herself) and a workout (weight training or Pilates) before the kids wake up. After getting them ready and taking them to school (her favorite part of the day), she’ll make another matcha, then settle in for a marathon of Zoom calls before spending the evening with the kids. She comes into the city once a week to meet with her team in person; The Mercer is her home away from home.

As an entrepreneur, she’s keen to take care of herself by consciously putting herself first, including upholding her boundaries. “When you do something that you really love, I think that is also a really beautiful commitment to yourself,” she says.

Having started her business at the tail end of 2019, Crowell worked tirelessly during the pandemic, when she also gave birth to her second child, Rosie. “I think back on the success of Saie, and a big part of it is that for two years I basically worked the amount that most people do in, like, six years,” she says. Her days were a balance of work and breastfeeding, and she only really left the house to go for walks. “After that, my immune system was a mess,” she adds. Working with holistic coach Jill Ketner Villa was her saving grace. Years later, Crowell says Villa is her “secret weapon” as a founder. Through her work with the practitioner, she’s developed her supplement ritual; she takes approximately 15 a day. Her assortment includes magnesium, zinc, iron, and vitamins C, A, and D—all housed in a beautiful leather pill case.

She’s also been meditating for 12 years. Nowadays, she loves Gabrielle Bernstein’s guided meditations, which last 10 to 20 minutes. “I get a lot of clarity out of it, and I find it to be the best way to calm anxiety.” Sleep, of course, is another tranquil practice; practice is the operative word because Crowell does a lot to ensure she gets a good night’s sleep. She takes magnesium or CBD before bed and uses the Truvaga device to stimulate her vagus nerve system and regulate her circadian rhythm. “Then I have my humidifier, face mask, earplugs, always a thick lip balm—that usually does it.” (The humidifier also makes its way to her weekly stays at The Mercer.)

Otherwise, she says going on a walk in nature helps her be her best self. “If I can fit a walk in between Zooms, that is a good day. I have my best ideas on walks.”

Crowell’s skin philosophy is appropriately minimalist, largely to suit her sensitive, dry, acne-prone skin. “I don’t use a ton of products, and I don’t use anything that’s super active. Generally, ‘less is more’ is what’s right for my skin, with a heavy focus on hydration.” As someone so knowledgeable and passionate about formulation, she’s gotten her skin-care regimen down to a science and has a couple of brands she knows work well for her skin.

First is Medik8, which she turns to for a suite of products: cleansers, a hydrating serum, and its Crystal Retinal. “Their retinol is the only retinol I can use. I use either level one, two, or three. I don’t really go above that. I also really love Sofie Pavitt’s line—it’s really wonderful if you have acne-prone skin.” Specifically, she gravitates toward the Omega Rich Moisturizer, Clean Clean Cleanser, and Mandelic Clearing Serum to spot-treat breakouts. Otherwise, she loves the Dr. Barbara Sturm toner for extra hydration, Alloy’s estrogen-powered face cream to boost skin’s plumpness, and Colorescience’s tinted mineral sunscreen if she’s skiing or in the sun, though her daily SPF is Saie’s Slip Tint. For extra protection, you’ll likely find her in a hat.

Crowell likes to keep her makeup routine quick. “I am not a makeup artist,” she says. “So all of our formulas are easy and effortless. I really love looking like myself.” Her five-minute regimen always starts with Saie’s Glowy Super Gel, which “gives [her] the most beautiful lit-from-within glow,” followed by Slip Tint for moisturizing coverage with sun protection. After a bit of Slip Tint concealer and a thoughtful dusting of the collection’s setting powder, she moves on to blush. At night she might layer the liquid Dew Blush and the powder SuperSuede blush, but for daytime it’s one or the other paired with the SuperSuede Baked Bronzer. Next, she brushes up her brows with gels by Kristie Streicher or Rare Beauty. Then she adds a mist of City Set setting spray, a few coats of Mascara 101, and her go-to lip combo (lip liner in shade Flirt, plus Glossybounce lip oil in shade Dip) for the finishing touches.

Hydration is key to keeping her thick, long brown hair, which can sometimes be dry, looking its best. “I don’t get a lot of haircuts, so I’m always hydrating the roots,” she says. Roz’s Milk Hair Serum comes with her everywhere, as does the brand’s Santa Lucia Styling Oil. “I really love her formulas,” she says. K18’s post-shower leave-in delivers molecular repair, while Arrey’s Not Today, Grey supplement slows early graying and supports fullness. “It’s 100% made a difference. When I stopped taking them, I noticed it.”

Another product she doesn’t leave home without is Dae’s styling wand, which comes in handy for taming flyaways. For her beloved beach waves, she swears by T3’s curling iron (specifically the 1-inch and 1.5-inch barrel sizes) and a bit of Dae’s styling cream.

Crowell is as thoughtful with her style as she is with her skin care and makeup. “I definitely believe in quality over quantity, so I wouldn’t say I buy a lot,” she says. When she does shop, however, she always asks herself if the item fits her “’90s, a little sporty, and minimalist” ethos. That said, she gravitates toward pieces from The Row, Khaite, and Kallmeyer or really special vintage finds. Most days, you’ll find her in jeans, a T-shirt, and Adidas sneakers—or Manolos to dress things up.

#6639. Green Bean Casserole

Green bean casserole belongs on every Thanksgiving spread. If you're scrambling to figure out how to perfect the classic side dish, look no further. I ditched the cream of mushroom soup for a homemade cream sauce and fresh sautéed mushrooms, but don’t worry—this dish is still super-simple to make. You only need 8 ingredients and a little over an hour to make it. Keep reading on for my top tips to make this classic side dish the best it can be:

What People Are Saying:"Once you have this, you'll never be able to go back to the Campbell's gray sodium laden sludge & soggy canned green beans recipe! This is amazing! Your whole family will change their minds about green bean casserole." - CyanCarrot

"This is the best green bean casserole I've ever made! It was easy enough that I could read it through twice, and then just make the dish without having to refer to the text of the recipe! It turned out great, my family ate it up! Without using a can of condensed soup, I could control the amount of salt...this made from scratch sauce tastes more mushroomy than a can of condensed soup!" - GreenBike

The very first step is blanching the green beans. This may seem a little fussy, but it's the best way to start cooking the beans to preserve their crispy texture and bright green color as they continue to bake in the oven. It's easy, but just requires a little setup. Cook the beans in salted water for a few minutes, then immediately transfer to a bowl of ice water to stop the cooking process. That's it!

On to the aromatics! This is what takes this casserole truly over the top. Cook sliced onions in butter until just starting to soften, anywhere from 7 to 10 minutes.

Add the mushrooms and let everyone get to know each other. Make it even better by adding some garlic; in my opinion, the more, the merrier. Your kitchen will smell amazing. Pour the aromatics into the bowl with the blanched beans.

Time for the sauce. No need to wipe out the pot, just melt a stick of butter, then toast the flour.

Slowly pour in the milk and whisk, letting the sauce come to a simmer, until nicely thickened. Be sure to taste for salt and pepper.

Add to bowl with beans, stir to combine, then transfer to a 13" x 9" baking dish. Spread in the dish, then bake until everything is bubbly, which should take just 30 minutes.

Time for the fried onions! Return to the oven and bake until the onions are just warmed through, about 5 minutes more.

The full list of ingredients and directions can be found in the recipe below.

Save some time (and oven space) on the busy holiday by making this Thanksgiving side ahead of time. Simply assemble the casserole (without the fried onions), cover tightly with plastic wrap, and refrigerate for up to 4 days or until you're ready to bake!

Some tips: Make sure to let it cool to room temperature before baking, or add a couple of minutes to your timer to adjust to the colder temperature. Whatever you do, DON'T forget to add the fried onions until after the first round of baking. If they're added before refrigerating, they'll get soggy instead of having that signature crispy crunch.

Store any leftovers in an airtight container (or cover your casserole dish with storage wrap) before refrigerating. This side will last up to 4 days refrigerated (look through our Thanksgiving leftovers ideas for some creative inspo for what to do with them), and leftovers can be reheated in the microwave or oven.

#6638. Everyone Wants a Garden-Ready Shoe

The rise of the extremely practical shoe, with a twist.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

It’s been a big year for shoes. In addition to the supreme reign of the It shoe, designers have fully entered a new era of experimentalism when it comes to footwear. And truly, nothing feels off-limits. Earlier this year, Schiaparelli took the cake with faux-taxidermy kitten heels, replete with shockingly lifelike snarling cat heads, eyes, whiskers, and tongues included. As did Prada, with Victorian-style boots covered in a collage of feathers and decadent, chandelier-studded low heels straight out of a Marie Antoinette fever dream. Fashion insiders and the masses got swept up in the hunt for Chanel’s ponyhair ballet flats, in a moment that felt like a new level of footwear obsession. Now, we have the runway-approved gardening shoe. Dior, TWP, and Public School have all shown their own takes on the look, each reinterpreting a classic garden clog.

Consider it the fashion equivalent of touching grass in the digital age. Gardening shoes have a clear connection to nature that is quite literally (and aesthetically) grounding. With their neutral beige tones and down-to-earth materials, they read as the opposite of the feathers, crystals, and animal-themed ponyhair of It shoes of the last few seasons. The garden-ready shoe combines the unapologetic high-fashion ugly-chic notion with the idea of practicality.

At Miu Miu fall/winter 2026, the runway was literally a mossy, grassy display ripe for stomping around in lug soles, and that’s what the models did, in bubble-soled slip-ons and chunky lace-up boots that almost looked like something you’d go hiking in, paired with technical nylon jackets and funky fedoras. Miu Miu called it “Clothes that are a method of self-preservation, a means of valuing that inside,” in the show notes. “They emblematize the individual, and in turn the identity of Miu Miu itself.”

There’s also undoubtedly a little bit of irony to this style. Most of the gardening shoes on the runway serve as a high-contrast answer to flashy, anything-but-toned-down runway garments. Take Loewe’s slip-on shoes with a rubberized toe that also looked ready for the task of planting biennials and perennials. They were styled with lush shearlings and near-neon orange latex dresses, the shoes put the classic jolie laide terminology to work. Shoes, after all, have for decades served as storytelling anchors within fashion collections. Elsewhere at Dior’s spring/summer 2027 show in Paris, brown suede shoes were capped off with rubberized toes and practical soles, worn with silk petal jackets and textural, couture-like miniskirts.

Simone Rocha just released its final collaboration with Crocs, including a Quick Trail Racer style that looks like something a real gardener would wear, only with Simone Rocha’s saccharine crystals and ruffles. Retailers have seen the look come and go: “The garden-like lug sole trend ebbs and flows in the background of footwear,” Moda Operandi’s Ryan Kleman, VP of buying, accessories, tells ELLE. “Ultimately, the appeal mostly stems from the idea of putting outfits together ‘with the wrong shoe.’ The garden shoe is the wrong shoe, in many ways. Otherwise, the shoe’s practicality and function underscore how easily customers can convert to them.”

#6637. ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Votes Are Reportedly “Significantly Lower” Than Last Season

Buuut the show is more popular than ever!

Dancing With the Stars might be airing one of its most-watched seasons yet, but apparently audience participation isn’t as high as usual. Despite fans tuning into the show week after week, turns out not that many people are picking up their phones to actually vote—or, at least, not as many as last season.

“The amount of votes coming in right now is significantly lower than this time last season because the Alix [Earle] vs Robert [Irwin] face-off sparked a ton of participation,” a source tells The Sun. “Producers are somewhat seeing that with Amber and Ezra and Tatyana. But there’s no one garnering that level of response this season and it shows with the numbers.”

Despite the low voting, Deadline reports that Dancing With the Stars’ opening episode saw a 24 percent increase from last season’s premiere. And fans even crashed the voting site! Meanwhile, Super Bowl Night saw the show get a “nine-year ratings high among adults 18-49” with 6.59 million live/same-day viewers.

Last year’s season 34 finale brought in 72 million votes (more than double the 32 million in season 33), and while votes are allegedly down this season, it’s possible next week could change things. Mostly because—for the first time ever!—ABC is airing Dancing With the Stars live on both the East Coast and the West Coast, meaning West Coast viewers will be able to watch the show as it airs and vote in real time. This should inevitably get the numbers up, so stay tuned…and remember to vote for your favorites during the finale next week at 8 p.m. ET!

#6636. Prince William and Kate Wear Traditional Indian Designs to Attend the Earthshot Prize Awards in Mumbai

The princess accessorized her intricate gown with Queen Elizabeth’s fantastic emerald tassel suite

The Prince and Princess of Wales paid homage to Indian culture as they attended the sixth annual Earthshot Prize Awards tonight in Mumbai.

After a busy day of engagements (and outfit changes), the couple made it to the Jio World Convention Centre to celebrate global change-makers who are actively working to solve urgent environmental problems. It was the first time the awards were held in India, and the event brought together around 5,000 global guests, world leaders, and celebrities.

And for the momentous evening, the British royals wore traditional Indian attire.

Prince William went for a rare all-black look, the highlight of which was a traditional velvet Bandhgala jacket by Indian designer Manish Malhotra. By his side, Princess Catherine—at her first Earthshot ceremony since 2022—was a vision in a vermilion-red anarkali gown custom-made for her by Indian designer Mahima.

The stunning long-sleeve piece had gold and green embroidery throughout, and Kate expertly accessorized it with jewelry from Queen Elizabeth II’s fabulous emerald tassel suite. Up top, she wore the late monarch’s luxurious diamond-and-emerald tassel drop earrings, while around her neck, the matching tassel necklace took the spotlight.

The suite is believed to have been gifted to Queen Elizabeth by Sheikh Zayed bin Sultan Al Nahyan of Abu Dhabi during the 1989 United Arab Emirates state visit to Britain. And through the years, the monarch took it out for several important occasions, including for the Return Dinner hosted in 2011 by President Barack Obama and first lady Michelle Obama at the end of their state visit to the United Kingdom.

In 2022, the queen loaned the suite to Kate, who wore the necklace and earrings to the state dinner hosted by the Governor General of Jamaica. Now, it’s become a shining standout in her royal jewelry collection.

#6635. The Attico Resort 2027

There’s something rather unnerving about a woman who is always perfectly turned out. The Attico’s spring collection takes the idea of composure and gives it a mischievous twist, imagining a cast of ladies whose immaculate appearances belie rather less conventional instincts. Think The Stepford Wives relocated to Milan, where good breeding, impeccable tailoring, and a well-managed domestic interior are still considered valuable social assets. At least until someone lets her hair down.

Sharp shoulders, sculpted jackets, corseted tops, and rigorously constructed dresses establish an almost 1940s-inspired formality, with a touch of Milanese perbenismo (bourgeois respectability). But the facade, as ever at The Attico, is deceptive. Crinkled fabrics undermine the precision, lingerie details escape their appointed boundaries, and florals introduce an unlikely note of romance. Beneath the buttoned-up exterior, a streak of kink is alive and well: pencil skirts that can be unbuttoned at the sides, thigh-high slits and revealing openings that turn propriety into a rather flexible concept.

There is a more grown-up, composed attitude extending into daywear and a cleaner approach to evening, where the emphasis shifts from decorative extravagance (feathers, sequins, fringe) to cut, silhouette, and construction. Even the most restrained looks, however, seem to harbor a secret. The Attico’s perfect woman may know how to behave. Whether she chooses to is another matter.

#6634. I Tried Taco Bell’s New FLAMIN’ HOT Menu Items To See If They Really Bring The Heat

One of these could've used a few extra sauce packets.

I'll try just about anything Taco Bell puts on its menu, and whatever I order usually ends up drenched in hot sauce. So when I heard the chain was adding a new FLAMIN' HOT offering (and bringing back a punchy fan favorite), I was curious whether they'd finally give me enough spice to leave those packets alone. I like my food hot enough to get a few beads of sweat forming on my nose, and with a name like FLAMIN' HOT, I don't think that's asking too much.

There were two contenders: the returning FLAMIN' HOT Grilled Cheese Burrito ($5.49), which I'd missed during its first run, and the brand-new FLAMIN' HOT Mini Taco Salad ($2.49). Both feature Fritos FLAMIN' HOT, so I figured I was in for some decent spice. Here's how that worked out.

I love a good taco salad, especially when the bowl itself is edible. But my first impression of this mini version was that it looked like a pretty ordinary Taco Bell salad. And after a few bites, I wasn't finding much to convince me otherwise.

You've got seasoned beef, refried beans, lettuce, tomatoes, cheddar, and Creamy Chipotle sauce tucked into a crispy flour tortilla bowl. Beneath everything was a layer of reddish rice, which Taco Bell says is seasoned with FLAMIN' HOT Fritos. I could definitely see the color, but whatever was making that rice spicy wasn't doing much for my taste buds.

And speaking of Fritos, I'm still trying to figure out where mine went. They're supposed to be in there, but I genuinely couldn't tell whether they'd been included in my particular serving. I would've loved more of the chipotle sauce, too, because the other ingredients seemed to overpower whatever heat was hiding underneath.

The shell itself was fine, although a little bland and greasy. I’m still pro-taco salads, but I just wish the spice had been more noticeable—especially with a name that makes such a big promise.

The returning burrito is stuffed with seasoned beef, spicy rice, crunchy FLAMIN' HOT Fritos, nacho cheese sauce, sour cream, and Creamy Chipotle sauce. And because Taco Bell clearly believes there's no such thing as too much cheese, the entire thing gets a three-cheese blend grilled onto the outside of the tortilla.

I took a pretty substantial first bite, making sure to get into those Fritos and all its FLAMIN’ HOT components. The nacho cheese had that gloriously gooey, movie-theater quality (complimentary), while the rest of the fillings worked together much better than they had in the salad. There's a lot happening inside this thing, and I was enjoying every bit of it.

Most importantly, I could actually taste the heat. There was a satisfying punch of spice, followed by that little tickle at the back of my throat that tells me something's doing its job. I wasn't desperately reaching for a drink, but the burrito had enough kick to make me happy.

I even went back for another bite of the mini taco salad afterward, and it tasted better with the burrito's lingering heat still in my mouth. Who knew it needed a little help from its menu companion?

Ultimately, the burrito is the one I'd order again. The mini taco salad has potential, and for $2.49, I wouldn't be opposed to giving it another shot. But next time, I'm bringing the extra hot sauce packets it should've saved me from needing.
